# Implementation phase 2: LVL010, the first gameplay level (and every level after it)

Phase 1 (`DECOMP_IMPL-1.md`, C1.0–C1.6) is done: boot, GAME/LVL000 load, the whole front end, audio. Phase 2 takes **New game** from the main menu into **LVL010**: the opening cutscene, then the hero in control and the first fight, then the rest of the level. It is written so that each later level is mostly "load it and fill the gaps", not a new project (§6).

Read first: `BLUEPRINT.md` (§6 milestones M4–M6, §7 verification, §8 pitfalls), `STYLE.md`, `HANDOFF.md`. Rules are the phase-1 rules (C16-BRIEF "Rules"): ported code from the EBOOT with address comments, the PS3 layout in the x86 parity build, both presets green, gate 1 and gate 2 after every checkpoint, Ghidra read-only for agents (names go through reports).

---

## 1. What we know (measured 2026-10-18)

### 1.1 The files
- `LVL010/LVL010.KP3` (161 MB, 33,313 objects), the four sector files `STR01000..STR01003.KP3` (1,764 / 1,349 / 2,487 / 1,236 objects), ten `nnLLC010.KP3` (6.3 MB each, one per language), `STREAMS/PS30..PS37.KCS` (cinematic streams), `SPEECH/*.RWS` (10 files; codec not decoded yet: probably ATRAC3, see C16 audio report).
- Videos: `MOVIE/01/PS3_010..019.VID` (the level's Bink cutscenes).
- `Shader/ps3/shfct.bin` (77 MB): precompiled RSX (NV40) Cg programs (3,872 programs by their `u_f44WorldViewProj` uniform); the game also has its own shader generator (`ShaderGen_*` 0xf41b0/0xf44c0, `ShaderVariantKey` 0xf1d48).

### 1.2 Classes
- LVL010 + its sectors instantiate **366 classes, 40,149 objects**. **132** of those classes have a ported Load (21,948 objects); **234 do not** (18,201 objects), 222 of them never seen in GAME/LVL000.
- All gameplay levels together use **403** classes: LVL010 covers 366; the other ten levels add only **37** between them (LVL020 5, 030 8, 040 13, 050 6, 060 19, 080 8, 090 11, 100 8, 110 10, 120 9; overlaps counted per level). Source: `outputs/remake/kp3-class-usage.json`.

### 1.3 Why New game crashes today
New game requests LVL010 sector 1 (5 → 9 → 1, `CKLoadingManager_UnloadSector` stubbed), then the loader starts `LVL010\LVL010.KP3` and crashes right after the "CreateArray of … is not ported yet" lines (reproduced headless with a scratch harness). Cause: `work/gen_kp3_stubs.py` generates **placeholder classes only for GAME, 00GLC, LVL000 and 00LLC000** (`work/scratch-impl1-loading`). The 233 LVL010-only classes have no factory, so the loader gets a null array and dereferences it before any of the level's data is read. Fixing that is the first step of C2.0; with placeholders the loader skips unknown objects to their end offsets exactly as it did for LVL000 during C1.2.

### 1.4 How much code (the new-game timeline `timeline-boot_to_level-20261014-224750.json`, stages from `outputs/remake/runtime-stages-newgame-lvl010.md`)
| Stage | First-run game functions | Already ported | Biggest areas |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| level_load (mode 1 with LVL010) | 1,201 | 162 (13%) | k2000/logic 427, Spyro2008/logic 141, graphical 85, scenegraph 76, Spyro2008/hooks 65, k2000/hooks 52, cinematics 49 |
| level_start (mode 4, game state 7) | 147 | 1 | Spyro2008/hooks 39, k2000/hooks 33, Spyro2008/logic 16, k2000/logic 13 |
| gameplay (mode 5, the cutscene and play) | 1,469 | 20 | Spyro2008/hooks 450 (hero/enemy), k2000/logic 270, k2000/hooks 96, cinematics 64, cameras 52 |
- The tracer hooks only game functions over 64 bytes (plus the largest small ones). The static call graph from those stages reaches about **540 / 300 / 1,630 more untraced game functions** and at least **220 / 40 / 130 RenderWare library functions** (a lower bound: RW render pipelines, skinning and animation run through callbacks the call graph does not follow).
- Order of magnitude: **3,000 traced + 2,500 untraced game functions + several hundred RW functions** for LVL010, against ~2,800 ported so far in total.

### 1.5 The opening cutscene
The timeline shows it is a **Bink video played by an in-level cinematic scene**: at level start `CKSrvCinematic_ShowLetterboxBars`, `CKCinematicScene_Start/Update`, `CKPlayVideoCinematicBloc_Start/Update/PlayNextVideo/UpdateSubtitles`. So the cutscene can play (video, subtitles, letterbox, skip) **before any 3D rendering exists**: a good first visible goal. In-engine cinematics (`CKStreamAnimCinematicBloc`, the KCS streams) come later in the level.

---

## 2. Principles for phase 2

1. **Load first, then run, then draw.** Every checkpoint keeps the level loadable headless and the gates green; nothing waits for 3D.
2. **Everything through the data:** the class list comes from the files (kp3-class-usage), the function list from timelines, the order from first-run time. No hand-made lists.
3. **One level path for all levels.** Nothing LVL010-specific in code: LVL010 is the test data. Each checkpoint adds a headless test that loads *every* level (see §6) so regressions in other levels show at once.
4. **Headless-testable logic, visual checks only where pixels matter.** Gameplay is checked by state (positions, flags, timers) against RPCS3 reads; rendering by eye (and later frame_compare).
5. **Keep the original's bugs and timing** (STYLE `BUG(original)`), the measured dt, the 60 Hz pacer.

---

## 3. Checkpoints

Each checkpoint lists: the work in order, what to port (addresses come from the timeline and the call graph at that time), and the exit test. Agent families as in phase 1 (one brief per checkpoint, families with disjoint files, the main session merges, verifies and applies names).

### C2.0 Any level loads without crashing (tooling + loader)
**Status (2026-10-18): done.** Every level (LVL010–LVL120) and every sector loads headless (`tests/c15/level_load_test.cpp`, ~8 s): 54 files, 420,195 objects, 0 mismatches in ported classes; 187,311 objects of 273 placeholder classes skipped (the C2.1 work list: `work/c20-level-load.json`). Placeholders come from each level's own class table (`work/level_class_keys.py` → `work/level-class-keys.json`; LVL010 has **382** classes, not the 366 the name-based count in §1.2 shows). Ported: the unload path, sector loads (`STRlllss.KP3`), SectorRequest, CKLevel sector streaming, Mode6, the level classes' Destroy/DestroyLevelData chain; level branches of CCloneManager (RpTeam readers) and CSpawnManager (its Init). `live_objects.py --level N` dumps any level's classes. **Not met yet:** New game reaches LVL010 mode 4, not 5 (the first mode-5 frame needs C2.2's level-start runtime: the HUD interfaces are placeholders). Report: `work/c20-report.json`.

1. `work/gen_kp3_stubs.py`: build the placeholder list from **every KP3 file's class list** (GAME, all GLC/LLC, all LVLnnn, all STRnnnnn: `kp3-class-usage.json` or `kp3_parse.py` over the game folder), not only LVL000's. Keep the "a hand-written header wins" rule. Add the class sizes for the native strides.
2. `CKLoadingManager`: port what the level load path needs that is stubbed: `UnloadSector` 0x283a8, `UnloadLevel` 0x28678, `UnloadGameObjects` 0x28b70 (the front end's objects go away before LVL010 loads), the mode-1 level branch (the level's `CKLevel` with its sector table), `CKGameLoop` mode 6/7/9 pieces still stubbed (`Mode6_LevelLoad` 0x20498, `Mode9_LevelExit` 0x213c8 full).
3. Sector files: `STR01000..` load through the same loader when the level asks for a sector (`SectorRequest_Execute` 0x34db0 is a stub): port the sector request and the sector load/unload around it.
4. Speech `RWS` and `.KCS` streams are only opened here (pre-open), not decoded.
5. Tools: `live_objects.py` and `objects_diff.py` take the class set of **any** file (today: tiers.json of LVL000 only), so an LVL010 dump can be the gate-2 reference.
6. **Exit:** headless test `level_load_test` (new): for **each** LVLnnn: boot, request the level's first spawn point, load until mode 4; 0 crashes, every object created, every end offset hit (unknown objects skipped and counted). New game in `dotd.exe` reaches LVL010 mode 5 with a black screen and no crash.

### C2.1 The Loads of the 234 LVL010 classes
**Status (2026-10-19): done.** All 273 placeholder classes have their constructors and Loads (seven agent families, brief `outputs/remake/C21-BRIEF.md`, lists `work/c21-packs/*.tsv`, reports `work/c21-report-*.json`, then a cleanup pass for the last 7 classes). All 11 levels and their sectors: 420,195 objects, **0 skipped, 0 mismatches**; both presets 20/20; front-end gate 2 OK; **LVL010 gate 2 OK** against `objects-lvl010_ingame-20261018-223707` with the native dump taken without the start sector (`level_load_test 10` with `DOTD_DUMP_LEVEL_SECTOR=0 DOTD_DUMP_LEVEL_OBJECTS=<pattern>`; the RPCS3 dump holds no sector objects in those slots). `objects_diff` now pairs file arrays to live windows by content and matches references by object identity (run-time-created instances no longer shift them); 2,351 runtime/mask rules from the reports are in `work/objects_diff_runtime.json`. Also: `CTriggerAction` parameters are a `CMultiData` union (x64-safe object refs), one `RwMatrixCreate` (rwcore; game code through `k2000/misc/Mat4.*`). Stubs carried to C2.2: the beacon 0x1000 write path, the cinematic channel-type slots other than Load, many PostLoads (listed per class in the reports). Ghidra proposals: `outputs/ability-analysis/proposals-c21.json`.

1. `work/load_packs.py` + `LOAD-BRIEF.md` (phase 1's tools) regenerated for the LVL010 class list, in tiers (Tier 0/A: plain fields; B: references and vectors; C/D: chunks, RW streams, custom readers).
2. Heavy families first (by object count and by what C2.2+ needs): scene graph and nodes (CAnimatedNode, CZoneNode, CSpawnNode, CSGPointLight, CSGHotSpot), geometry (CSkinGeometry, CMultiGeometry, CMultiGeometryBasic, CClone), animation (CAnimationDictionary, CKAnmKeyFrameController, CKBlenderController), collision volumes (CKAABB, CKOBB, CKAACylinder, CGround, CDynamicGround, CKQuadTreeLeaf), hooks (CKHk*: hero, enemies, doors, climb zones, save points...), groups (CKGrp*), components (CK*Cpnt), cinematics (CKCinematicScene, CKCinematicSceneData, the 25 bloc classes, CKStartDoor/CKEndDoor), cameras (CKCamera, CKCameraClassicTrack3, CKCameraSector, CKAnimatedCamera), FX (the *NodeFx/*FxData pairs), logic (CKTrigger, counters, comparators, CKSekens*, CKLogicalAnd/Or), abilities/combat data (CKS08HeroCombo, CKS08HeroBlow, CKDamage family, the element attack classes), HUD (CKS08InGameInterface Left/Center/Right), audio (CKMusicPlayList, CKMarkedSoundDico, CKStreamObject).
3. The XXL2 symbols name most K-engine classes and members (`xxl2_syms.py`): use them for struct fields first (STRUCT-BRIEF), then the Loads.
4. **Exit:** gate 1 (0 skipped objects in LVL010 and its sectors, every class's end offset), gate 2 against an LVL010 object dump (captures §5, B) with the runtime fields listed.

### C2.2 Level start: from New game to the hero standing in LVL010 (game state 7, mode 5)

**Goal.** New game → Start Story Mode → Single Player leaves the front end, loads LVL010, starts the level and runs gameplay frames (loop mode 5, game state 7) without crashing, with the level's objects in the state the original has right after control is given. No drawing, no cutscene playback (C2.3), no hero control (C2.6): only the start-up and the per-frame updates the original runs.

**Work list (generated, in the original's order):** `work/c22-packs/level-start.tsv` (`python work/c22_pack.py` regenerates it from `outputs/remake/timeline/timeline-lvl010_newgame-20261018-204409.json`; columns: step, time, phase, address, Ghidra name, done = ported, TU file). Measured 2026-10-19:

| step | what | functions | ported |
|---|---|---:|---:|
| 1-exit-front-end | leaving the front end (phases gm6-mode9 / gm0-mode9: Stop, sector unloads) | 58 | 17 |
| 2-level-load | the LVL010 load (C2.0/C2.1; mostly done; the rest are PostLoads, see step 3) | 1,142 | 747 |
| 3-level-start | level start (phase gm7-mode4: Start, ResetRuntime, hook/group inits) | 147 | 1 |
| 4-first-frames | gameplay frames before the opening cutscene's window | 889 | 10 |

**Where it stops today (2026-10-19, reproduced with the harness below):** the load works, then at mode 4 `CKS08GameManager::Start` → `ResetRuntime` (CKS08GameManager_2.cpp ~line 483) switches the interface state machine to "no menu" (0x24); its leave call runs `InterfaceStateDispatch` for the **still current front-end menu 0x23** (the Start Player menu) and dereferences the LVL010 menu table entry 0x23, which is null → crash. Cause: in the original, leaving the front end runs `IKGameManager_Stop` 0x13e08 and `CKS08GameManager_Stop` 0x4f22e8 (and `KBase730C_ClearSlotsAndClose_maybe` 0x518f28 on the open menus) in mode 9; in dotd `CDefaultGameManager::Stop` (vt+0x68 0x1a788) is still an empty `STUB(C1.6)` in `k2000/logic/CDefaultGameManager.h`, so the menus are never closed. Step 1 fixes exactly this.

#### Tools you use in every step
- **Build + all tests:** `cmd /c work\wt_build.cmd <repo root>` (x86 parity preset, PS3 layout, runs ctest with the game data). Once per step also `cmd /c work\wt_build.cmd <repo root> x64`. Both must stay 100% green.
- **The harness:** `dotd/tests/c15/newgame_test.cpp` boots, presses START, then Cross three times through the menus (main menu → game menu → start player) like a player, and steps frames, printing every loop-mode / game-state change. It is skipped unless `DOTD_NEWGAME_TEST=1`. Run it directly: set `DOTD_USRDIR` (the game's USRDIR, see `work/wt_build.cmd`), `DOTD_KP3_DATA=<repo>\work\scratch-impl1-loading`, `DOTD_AUDIO=off`, `DOTD_NEWGAME_TEST=1`, then `dotd\build\windows-msvc-x86-parity\Debug\newgame_test.exe`.
- **Crash stacks with line numbers:** `"C:\Program Files (x86)\Windows Kits\10\Debuggers\x86\cdb.exe" -G -y <Debug dir> -c ".lines -e; g; kpn 12; q" <newgame_test.exe>` (same environment variables; Windows paths). The top frames name the function to port next.
- **The original code:** Ghidra HTTP at `http://127.0.0.1:8089` via `work/ghttp.py` (`from ghttp import get`; `get('/decompile_function', address='0x...')`, `get('/disassemble_function', ...)`, `get('/get_xrefs_to', ...)`). Read-only. **Read the assembly** for float maths, byte/word swaps, bit fields and branch conditions; the decompiler drops things.
- **Original names:** `python work/xxl2_syms.py find <Name>` / `vtable <Class>` / `dis <Func> --class <Class>` (XXL2 has the same K engine with symbols).
- **Gates after every step:**
  1. ctest green on both presets (includes `level_load_test`: every level still loads with 0 mismatches);
  2. front-end gate 2: `python work/objects_diff.py work/native-objects.json outputs/remake/census/objects-frontend_lvl000-20261017-144833.json.gz` → OK;
  3. from step 4 on, the in-game gate (step 4).
- **Style:** `dotd/docs/STYLE.md` (address comment `// 0x00xxxxxx Name` above every ported function; tick the TU checklist line `//   [x] 0x...`; exact float ops; `BUG(original):` for original bugs; keep each file's line endings). New stubs must say why: `STUB(C2.3)` / `STUB(C2.6)` etc., and must not change state the original changes before that checkpoint.

#### Step 1. Leave the front end like the original (stage 1-exit-front-end)
1. Port `CDefaultGameManager::Stop` (vt+0x68, IKGameManager_Stop 0x13e08) and `CKS08GameManager_Stop` 0x4f22e8 (the override; find its vtable slot with `xxl2_syms.py vtable` and the vtable in `CKS08GameManager.h`). It closes the open menus: `KBase730C_ClearSlotsAndClose_maybe` 0x518f28, `CKInterfaceButtonDouble_Close` 0x523028, `CKInterfaceCheckBox_Close` 0x531ba0 (check which of these already exist in `Spyro2008/logic/KBase730C.cpp`, `k2000/logic/CKInterfaceButtonDouble.cpp`, `CKInterfaceCheckBox.cpp`).
2. Port the rest of the stage-1 rows of the work list that are not done, in time order: the `*_Stop` / `*_SectorUnload` / `*_PostSectorUnload` of the services (`CKServiceManager_Stop` 0x232a0 and the per-service ones), `CKLevel_SectorUnload` 0x2f718 / `PostSectorUnload` 0x2f680, `CKGameLoop_SectorUnload` 0x21130 / `PostSectorUnload` 0x20150, `CKGraphic_Stop` 0x5a128, `CKSoundManager_SectorUnload` 0x113248, `CKS08GameManager_SectorUnload` 0x4eeab8, `CKS08GameManager_PickRandomTipText_maybe` 0x4ec9b8 (the loading tip), `CKTriggerDomain_ResetTriggersAndEnableState_maybe` 0x2388f8. Each is called from a loop/manager function that is already ported: replace the stub or the missing call there.
3. **Exit:** with `DOTD_NEWGAME_TEST=1`, `newgame_test` gets past `CKS08GameManager::ResetRuntime` (the interface state is "no menu" 0x24 before the level starts). Commit: `dotd C2.2: leaving the front end (game manager and services Stop, sector unloads)`.

#### Step 2. Level start (stage 3-level-start, phase gm7-mode4)
1. Go through the 147 stage-3 rows in time order. Most are `CKHk*` / `CKGrp*` `Init`, `Reset`, `LevelReset`, `PostLoad` late parts and the game manager's in-game start; their classes are all ported (C2.1), so you add the method bodies to the existing class files (the TU file column says where).
2. The game manager's in-game parts left as stubs in C1.6 belong here: in `Spyro2008/logic/CKS08GameManager_2.cpp` / `_3.cpp` search `STUB(C1.6)` / `DOTD_STUB_ONCE`: `IKGameManager_ResetRuntimeImpl: the in-game start` (the pause fade CColorizedScreenFx at +0x490, `SetActiveCameraIndex_maybe` vt+0x294), `CKS08GameManager_GetControlledHeroCount` (the hero group, now ported as `CKGrpS08Hero`), `CKS08GameManager_StateInGame_maybe` 0x5043a8 (game state 7), `CKS08GameManager_Update`'s in-game HUD part, `IKGameManager_Update`'s trigger signals (`QueueTriggerSignals`), hero death / respawn sector requests (port the code, they only run when their flags are set).
3. Also in this step: `CKSector_QueueEnterEvent_maybe`'s `QueueTriggerSignals` (`k2000/logic/CKSector.cpp`, `STUB(C2.2)`), `CKGameModule_vf94 ApplyLeft: SetGameState` (`k2000/logic/CKGameModule.cpp`), and the other `STUB(C2.2)` markers the C2.0/C2.1 agents left (37 sites; `grep -rn "STUB(C2.2)" dotd/src`): CKLevel, CKGameLoop, CKSrvBeacon (the 0x1000 beacon write path), KBase1944, CKGrpSquad release paths, the PostLoads of CAnimatedNode, CSpawnNode, CSparkNodeFx, CGlowNodeFx, CSGPointLight, CKSpawnPool, CSpawnPoolRenderParam, CKSurfaceParticlesRendable, CKAnimatedFx, CKAnimationMarkContainer, CKBundle, CKDetectorBase/Event, CKDetectorMusic, CKCinematicStreamCpnt (channel-type slots), CKFlaggedPathCinematicBloc's state handlers, RpHAnim (`RtAnimInterpolatorCreate`), RwFrameList (`RwFrameAddChild` with the dirty list). Port each from the original; most PostLoads resolve references and set flags.
4. **Exit:** `newgame_test` reaches loop mode 5 with game state 7 and runs at least 10 frames. Commit per group of classes: `dotd C2.2: level start (<classes>)`.

#### Step 3. The first gameplay frames (stage 4-first-frames)
1. Make `newgame_test` run **600 frames** in mode 5 / game state 7. Loop: run it under cdb, port the function at the top of the crash stack (and what it needs), rebuild, repeat. Use the stage-4 rows to see what the original runs per frame and in which order; port whole functions, not just the crashing line.
2. The opening cutscene starts in these frames (`CKSrvCinematic`, `CKCinematicScene_Start/Update`, `CKPlayVideoCinematicBloc_*`): port their **state logic** here so the game state machine behaves as in the original (scene started, blocking flags, letterbox state), but the video playback and the skip prompt are C2.3 (`STUB(C2.3)` with the reason).
3. The hero's per-frame update (`CKHkS08Hero_*`, hundreds of functions) is C2.6. Here only what runs while the hero stands still during the cutscene/tutorial: if a hero function is needed only for movement or input, give it a `STUB(C2.6)` early return **only after checking in the assembly that it changes nothing the standing hero needs** (position, animation state, group links); say so in the comment.
4. Add to `newgame_test`: after 600 frames, the dump hook of `level_load_test` (`Kp3_DumpObjects(path)` when `DOTD_DUMP_LEVEL_OBJECTS` is set; copy the code from `dotd/tests/c15/level_load_test.cpp` ~line 147), and assertions: loop mode 5, game state 7 (`m_gameState.m_ui8Current == 7`), current sector = the spawn sector (3 for LVL010 in C2.0's results).
5. **Exit:** 600 frames without a crash on both presets; `newgame_test` passes with `DOTD_NEWGAME_TEST=1`. Commit.

#### Step 4. The in-game state equals the original's
1. Take the native dump at the end of `newgame_test` (`DOTD_DUMP_LEVEL_OBJECTS=<path>`) and compare with the RPCS3 dump taken right after control was given: `python work/objects_diff.py <native.json> outputs/remake/census/objects-lvl010_ingame-20261018-223707.json.gz`. Unlike C2.1's gate (taken before level start, sector unloaded), this one runs after level start, so the runtime fields of C2.1 should now **match** for the classes whose start-up you ported: work through the differences class by class, biggest first (CKHkS08Hero, CKHkS08Enemy, CKGrpS08Hero, CKGrpSquad, CKS08GameStructure, CKS08GameManager), port what writes them, and remove the matching entries from `work/objects_diff_runtime.json` when they match (keep entries that are still written later in gameplay, with the reason).
2. The hero position: CKHkS08Hero is in the live dump twice (Spyro and Cynder); after this step its fields that the level start writes (position/orientation in its node frame, ground contact, movement mode `+0x1808` = 0 ground) must equal the live values.
3. Note the live dump was taken while the tutorial was shown and the game manager was in game state 7 / menu 0x10; small timer differences (frame counters, animation times) are expected: list them as runtime fields with "time-dependent" as the reason.
4. **Exit:** `objects_diff` against `objects-lvl010_ingame-20261018-223707` reports only listed runtime fields; both presets green; `level_load_test` green; front-end gate 2 OK. Make `newgame_test` unconditional (remove the `DOTD_NEWGAME_TEST` guard) so it runs in ctest from now on. Update this section's status line and `HANDOFF.md`, commit.

**Status (2026-10-24): C2.2 done.** New game takes the front end into LVL010 (mode 9 -> 1 -> 4 -> 5) and runs 600 frames in loop mode 5 / game state 7 with the spawn sector current; `newgame_test` is unconditional now (ctest, both presets 22/22; it leaves with `_Exit` because the engine's load threads are still joinable). Work came from the families svc, hooks, spyro, gm (brief `outputs/remake/C22-BRIEF.md`, reports `work/c22-report-*.json`), three diff families, a flake fix (Rt2d CTM stack race: render lock in `CKFlashUI::PostLoad` and the message overlay, marked PORT) and five strict families. **Gate 2 in game** (native dump: `DOTD_DUMP_LEVEL_OBJECTS=work/native-lvl%03d-ingame.json` with `newgame_test`; `python work/objects_diff.py [--strict] work/native-lvl010-ingame.json outputs/remake/census/objects-lvl010_ingame-20261018-223707.json.gz`): OK in the default and in `--strict` mode (a native 0xcdcdcdcd is no longer taken for a heap pointer), every remaining difference is a rule in `work/objects_diff_runtime.json` naming its writer and checkpoint. Do not run `rules_needed.py --prune` on the in-game dump alone (the front-end gate needs some rules). **Rules that hide real start-up differences** (to revisit): CKFlashUI +0xf4/+0xf8 (queued Flash messages live), the boss HUD and CKMenuMessageBox phase-1 effects (`KBase730C_HandleStatePhase`), CKGrpSquad +0xc (the 4th squad awake live), CKS08GameManager +0x130/+0x26c/+0x300/+0x380/+0x408/+0xdb8/+0xdd8/+0xe9c (heap pointers, unexplained), CKS08FightZoneCpnt +0x14, CKTransformationCpntUpdater +0x08 (starter not found), hook `OnActivate`/`OnDeactivate` (vt+0xa0/+0xa4, ~60 classes, not ported). **Not ported (stubs with a checkpoint):** hero ground probe, input/focus (C2.6), `UpdateFrame` follow camera (C2.6), `CKHkS08Clamp_Start`, carrier hero, detectors, enemy AI `CKGrpSquad` SetPhase (C2.7), `CMaterial_PostLoad`, shadow caster release, trail geometry (C2.4), animation blends (C2.5), the cutscene functions (C2.3), pause/hero-death states, `CKSrvPathFinding::Update` (C2.7), the `CKPxBody` loop in physics `Stop`.

#### Rules for whoever implements this
- Never edit game files; Ghidra is read-only (put proposed names in `work/c22-names.json` with evidence); the 5 GB memory cap stays; never page Ghidra's `/list_functions`.
- One step at a time, build green between commits. If a step is too big for one session, stop at a green build with a commit and a short note at the end of this section saying where you are.
- Do not "fix" the original: no extra null checks or error handling the PS3 code does not have (a crash means something earlier is missing, as in step 1).
- Do not reorder or rename existing fields without updating every user; the x86 parity build's `DOTD_ASSERT_OFFSET` checks must keep passing.
- Bash heredocs in this environment eat backslashes: write C++ with the editor tools, not `cat <<EOF`.

### C2.3 The opening cutscene (no 3D needed)

**Goal.** After New game, LVL010's launch scene plays like the original: the letterbox appears, input is blocked, the opening video `MOVIE/01/PS3_019.VID` plays with its sound and subtitles and can be skipped, then the in-engine part of the scene runs its logic (camera, animation, dialogue lines with subtitles, teleports, path finding, pauses) and ends; the letterbox goes away and the player gets control. The in-engine part **runs its logic only**: the 3D drawing of it is C2.4/C2.5 (the screen stays black there, with letterbox and subtitles). The tutorial that follows control is not part of C2.3.

Without it, play nothing but keep the line timing exactly (the lines' durations decide when the bloc moves on: find where the duration comes from, the stream header or the sekens data).

#### Rules
Those of C2.2 ("Rules for whoever implements this") apply unchanged: read-only game files and Ghidra (proposed names to `work/c23-names.json`), the memory cap, one step at a time with green builds and commits, no "fixes" of the original, no layout changes without updating users, editor tools instead of bash heredocs for C++.

### C2.4 The 3D renderer foundation (the static world)

**Goal.** `dotd.exe` draws LVL010's static world (the level's and the start sector's non-skinned geometry) from the game's own cameras, with the game's materials, textures, shaders, lights and fog, through the OpenGL backend. Skinned characters (the hero, enemies: 19 skinned geometries) are C2.5; post effects (bloom, depth of field, HDR, distortion) are the last, optional step here or C2.4b. The camera's *behaviour* is C2.6: here the camera matrix is whatever the ported game code computes (the cinematic cameras of the opening scene, then the camera state at control).

**Work list (generated):** `work/c24-packs/render.tsv` (`python work/c24_pack.py`, needs Ghidra): the render path's entry points and everything they reach in the static call graph up to depth 4, by area (frame, geometry, rwgeom, shader, material, light), with done (ported) and first_seen_ms (the original ran it in the LVL010 timeline; game code only, the tracer does not hook library code). Measured 2026-10-24: frame 126 functions (31 ported), geometry 13 (1), rwgeom 202 (0), shader 24 (0), material 9 (1), light 2 (0). The call graph misses virtual calls and the RenderWare pipeline callbacks: follow those by hand (vtable slots; the callback tables `InstallPSGLMeshCallbacks` 0xff960 installs).

**What the data is (`python work/c24_geometry_scan.py LVL010`, USRDIR in `DOTD_USRDIR`):** 1,103 atomics with 1,103 geometries, 1,103 materials, 1,120 texture chunks, 1,103 bin-mesh chunks (0x50e). Geometries: **620 empty** (format flags 0: placeholders, particle/PTank holders), **111 in RenderWare's generic format** (flags 0x5e / 0x77), **372 with PS3 native data** (chunk 0x510, 7.4 MB: pre-instanced vertex/index buffers in the PS3 driver's layout; flags 0x5f / 0x77 / 0x4f / 0x5e / 0x76, one texture set). Header totals: 204,107 triangles, 134,862 vertices. Also 56 MatFX chunks (0x120), 19 skin (0x116, C2.5), 23 HAnim (0x11e, read in C2.1).

**What the original does to draw a frame (addresses for the steps below):**
- **Frame:** `RenderGameFrame` 0xa1c08 (C1.4 ported its 2D part; the world and post stages are `STUB(M2)` in `k2000/render/CRenderer.cpp`), `ExecuteRenderStages4CTo74` 0x9ff70, `ExecuteRenderStages38To48` 0xa1408, `ExecuteRenderStage78` 0xa1a78 (and `ExecuteRenderStages7CTo90` 0xa0ac8, done), `SyncSceneCamerasAndClipPlanes` 0xa04e8, `BeginRenderCameraUpdate` 0xf7fd8, `CRenderer_StageApplySceneStates_maybe` 0x9f2b0, `ApplySceneRenderStatesAndFog` 0x9ed38 (`FogManager_GetMainDensity_maybe` 0x12bee0 / `GetFarDensity_maybe` 0x12bda0), `UpdateActiveSectorWorld_maybe` 0x1fb9e0, the render-queue sort (`CompareRenderItemsPriorityThenFarToNear` 0x9f5c0; the libc qsort is a `STUB(crt)`), `RpWorldCreate` for the renderer (+0x34, `STUB(M2)`).
- **Geometry:** `CGeometry_UpdateAndExtract` 0x886d8 (done), `CGeometry_Render` 0x88e28 (vt+0x5c; `STUB(C1.4)` in `k2000/geometry/CGeometry.h`), `CGeometry_SetupMaterialShader_maybe` 0xbb828, the MatFX setup (`RpMatFXAtomicEnableEffects` 0x698038, `_rpMatFXPipelineAtomicSetup_maybe` 0x69a9c8).
- **RenderWare PS3 driver (rwgeom):** `RpGeometryNativeStreamReadCB_maybe` 0x6b3ce0 → `RpGeometryNativeStreamRead_maybe` 0x6b6fb0 (+ `StreamReadNativeHeader64_maybe` 0x6bc1c8), `RwPSGL_CreateMeshInstanceData_maybe` 0x6bb940, `RwPSGL_ReinstanceMeshes_maybe` 0x6baec0, `DispatchPSGLPipelineMeshes` 0x6bafc8, `DrawPSGLDefaultPipelineMesh` 0x6bb340, `RwPSGL_DrawMeshList_maybe` 0x69b118, `DrawPSGLMeshPasses` 0xffe78, `PreparePSGLAtomicMeshShaders` 0xff778, `InstallPSGLMeshCallbacks` 0xff960. Below them the PSGL GL wrappers (`PSGL_glGenBuffers_maybe` 0x5eaea0, `PSGL_glBindBuffer` 0x5eb328, `PSGL_glBufferData` 0x5eb480, `PSGL_glMapBuffer` 0x5eb7f0, `PSGL_glDrawRangeElements` 0x61f0e0, `PSGL_glDrawArrays` 0x61eff0, …): these are **not ported**, they become calls into `platform/gfx` (the same OpenGL concepts).
- **Shaders:** `ShaderVariantKey_BuildForMesh_maybe` (0xf1508 / 0xf1c90 / 0xf1c98), `ShaderVariantList_*`, `ShaderGen_CollectVertexUniforms_maybe` 0xf44c0 / `CollectPixelUniforms_maybe` / `CollectExternFragments_maybe`, `CShaderManagerPSGL_GetShaderModeForKey_maybe` 0xf8840, `FindOrCreatePrograms_maybe` 0xfd3f8, `CreateAtomicPipeline` 0xff888, `LoadVertexShader_maybe` 0xff2f0 / `LoadPixelShader_maybe` 0xfdf90 / `LoadShaderFctFile_maybe` 0xfd110 / `LoadShaderCache_maybe` 0xfaf98, 0xfb7d8, `ShaderManagerPSGL_TryAssignOverride_maybe` 0xf8168, `FillSkinBoneMatrixPaletteUniform` 0xeea50 (C2.5).
- **The shader source is in the EBOOT** (game data, read at run time, never copied into the repository): the game's Cg "uber-shader", about 94 KB of text in 14 blocks (the PS3 build at 0xc26750..0xc2af22+21292, a second copy at 0xc19848..; `#pragma pack_matrix(row_major)`, `struct SVertexInput` at 0xab5b18, the uniform tables `DeclareUniform(...)` at 0xc1d36c / 0xc1d53b, helpers from 0xc1dec8), switched by **48 `extern*` defines** (`externTransform`, `externWeightNb`, `externTexCoordNb`, `externVertexColor`, `externDirLight`, `externPointLight`, `externSpotLight`, `externFogType`, `externSpecular`, `externUseNormalMap`, `externUseEnvMap`, `externMATERIALFX_*` (NONE, ENV, UVTRANSFORM, DUALUVTRANSFORM, DUALPASS, NORMAL, POM, REFRACT, REFRACT_ENV, ALPHA_SORT), `externShadowEnable`, `externPS3`, the post passes `externBloom`, `externGaussianBlur5x5`, `externDownSample4x4/16x16`, `externRenderDepthOfField`, …). The game builds the define list from a `ShaderVariantKey`, then compiles; `Shader/ps3/shfct.bin` (77 MB) is only the cache of the compiled NV40 programs, which a PC cannot use.
- **Materials and lights:** `CMaterial_PostLoad` 0xbe348 (stubbed in C2.2), the CMaterial render-state handlers, `CLightManager_PreUpdate` 0x9afe0, `LightZone_UpdateWorldBounds_maybe` 0x98f18, `ApplyLightingZone` 0x994e8 (`STUB(M2)` in CRenderer.cpp), `CLightSet`, `CSGPointLight`, fog (`CKCameraFogDatas`, `CFogBoxNodeFx` with its PTank particles: `STUB(C2.4)` in its PostLoad).

#### Tools
The C2.2 tool list applies (build, `newgame_test`, cdb, Ghidra, XXL2, gates, STYLE). Plus:
- **Screenshots:** `DOTD_SCREENSHOT=path_%d.png:frame,frame` (C1.4) saves the internal 1280×720 target at given frames; look at the PNGs with an image viewer (or the Read tool).
- **Comparison with RPCS3:** `python work/frame_compare.py dotd.png rpcs3.png --panels --out cmp.png` (C1.6).
- **RenderWare 3.7.0.2 source** (local `rwsrc-v3.7.0.2-master` checkout, read-only): `world/` has the generic readers (`RpGeometryStreamRead`, `RpMaterialStreamRead`, `_rpMeshRead` bin mesh, `RpAtomicStreamRead`, plugin chunks), `plugin/matfx`, `plugin/skin2`; port from it where it matches the PS3 code, check the assembly where the PS3 build differs (the native data reader and everything `PSGL` have no source).

#### Step 1. Read the world geometry for real (no drawing yet)
1. Replace the C1.3 shim `RwShim_RpAtomicStreamRead` (`Renderware/rpworld/RpAtomic.cpp`: it keeps the 0x14 chunk as a blob) with the real readers: `RpAtomicStreamRead`, `RpGeometryStreamRead` (struct, morph targets, generic vertices/triangles/UVs/prelit colours/normals when not native), the material list and `RpMaterialStreamRead` (colour, texture by name through the level's dictionaries, `FindTextureByName` C1.6), the bin mesh (0x50e), the plugin extensions: MatFX (0x120: env map, UV transform, dual pass), skin (0x116: read and keep for C2.5), and **the PS3 native data (0x510)** from `RpGeometryNativeStreamReadCB_maybe` 0x6b3ce0 / `RpGeometryNativeStreamRead_maybe` 0x6b6fb0 / `StreamReadNativeHeader64_maybe` 0x6bc1c8: port its header and buffer layout exactly (keep the vertex/index bytes as the file has them plus the header that describes them: stride, attribute offsets/types, index format, mesh ranges).
2. Extend the C1.5 data model (`Renderware/rpworld/RpGeometry.h`, `RpAtomic.h`) instead of making a second one; keep PS3 layout asserts for fields game code reads.
3. Users of the old blob (`atomic->streamed`) must be moved to the real fields (grep `streamed`); CSkinGeometry, CMultiGeometry, CParticleGeometry, CClone included.
4. **Exit:** a new test `tests/c15/geometry_read_test.cpp` loads LVL010 (as `level_load_test` does) and counts what it read: the same numbers as `c24_geometry_scan.py` prints (1,103 geometries; 620 empty, 111 generic, 372 native; the triangle and vertex totals; 372 native-data chunks with 7,420,100 bytes (the scan said 371 / 7,419,096: its find() of the bytes 10 05 00 00 matched the length field of the geometry at file offset 0x7930028, whose chunk is exactly 0x510 bytes long; fixed, see the test's header)); `level_load_test` still 0 mismatches over all 11 levels (end offsets unchanged), gate 2 front end and in game OK, both presets green.


**Step 1 status (2026-10-24): done.** `Renderware/rpworld/RpGeometryStream.cpp` has the real readers (RW 3.7 source checked against the PS3 code): `RpAtomicStreamRead` 0x69ff28 (ClumpAtomicStreamRead without frame and geometry lists), `RpGeometryStreamRead` 0x6a2248 (struct, generic prelit/uv/triangles, morph targets, material list, plugin chunks), `_rpMaterialListStreamRead` 0x6a4ec8, `RpMaterialStreamRead` 0x6a40c0 (colour, surface properties, the texture by name through `FindTextureByName`, MatFX 0x120 and the right-to-render chunk 0x1f), `_rpMeshRead` 0x6a6050 (bin mesh 0x50e), the native data 0x510 (`RpGeometryNativeStreamRead_maybe` 0x6b6fb0: struct word 0xc, header size, header = word 0 {serial, mesh count} + 0x78 bytes per mesh, then the first mesh's 32-bit index buffer and vertex buffer; header and buffers are PS3 big-endian and stay so: `RpNativeGeometry` at `RpGeometry::repEntry`, the header words converted into `RpNativeMesh` whose fields are named after `DrawPSGLDefaultPipelineMesh` and `RwPSGL_ComputeInterleavedVertexLayout_maybe`), the skin chunk 0x116 (kept as bytes, C2.5), and the atomic's right-to-render chunk (`RpAtomic::rightsPluginId`: 0x116 for the 19 skinned atomics). #### Step 2. Shaders: the uber-shader in GLSL
1. Port the variant selection: `ShaderVariantKey_BuildForMesh_maybe` (three entries), `ShaderVariantList_*`, `CShaderManagerPSGL_GetShaderModeForKey_maybe`, `ShaderGen_Collect{Vertex,Pixel}Uniforms_maybe` / `CollectExternFragments_maybe` (the list of `extern*` defines and uniforms a key turns on). Into `k2000/render/ShaderManager*` (or the existing CShaderManagerPSGL files; `grep -rn ShaderManager dotd/src`).
2. Write `platform/gfx/cg_to_glsl.*` (new platform code, not a port): at run time read the Cg blocks from `EBOOT.elf` (the file is in USRDIR; take the offsets above, verify each block's first bytes), run a C-preprocessor pass with the variant's `#define extern... ` list (the Cg uses `#define/#ifdef/#ifndef/#undef/#error` and function-like macros like `DeclareUniform(x_enum, x_type, x_name, x_register, x_count)`), then translate the Cg subset to GLSL 3.30 core: `float2/3/4` → `vec2/3/4`, `float4x4` → `mat4` with `row_major` handled (Cg `mul(v, M)` with row-major packing becomes `M * v` on transposed upload, or upload row-major and swap: decide once and test with a known matrix), `half*` → `float*`, `tex2D/texCUBE` → `texture`, `lerp` → `mix`, `frac` → `fract`, `saturate(x)` → `clamp(x,0,1)`, `rsqrt` → `inversesqrt`, `fmod` (sign rules differ: implement Cg's), semantics (`: POSITION`, `: TEXCOORD0`, `: COLOR`, `: register(c..)`) → `in/out` declarations and uniform names, `struct` inputs/outputs → flattened varyings, `static const` → `const`. Keep a cache of compiled programs per variant key in memory (and optionally on disk under the build folder, never in the repository).
3. If one construct is too awkward to translate mechanically, hand-write that one function in GLSL in the translator's override table with the Cg's text location in a comment; never invent shading the Cg does not have.
4. **Exit:** a test enumerates the variant keys of every LVL010 material/geometry (`ShaderVariantKey_BuildForMesh` over all loaded CGeometry objects), translates and compiles them all with GL (hidden window, as `gfx_c16_test` does): 0 compile/link errors; print how many distinct variants LVL010 needs.


**Step 2 status (2026-10-24): done.** *Cg source:* the game does not compile Cg on the PS3 at run time (`LoadVertexShader_maybe` 0xff2f0 / `LoadPixelShader_maybe` 0xfdf90 read the NV40 binaries from `shfct.bin`; `ShaderGen_CompileCgSource_maybe` 0xfe7b8 runs the embedded `cgc` only to fill that cache). The text is two NUL-terminated blocks in `EBOOT.elf` (file offsets, not addresses): the **vertex** program source (shaderdefine + commonshadertools + `VertexShaderMain`) at 0xc26750 (39,678 bytes, first line `#pragma pack_matrix (row_major)`), the **pixel** program source at 0xc19848 (52,999 bytes, first line `#ifndef externBasicShader`); the small basic-shader texts at 0xab5b00 are not used. `platform/gfx/cg_to_glsl.cpp` `LoadGameSources` verifies both. *Platform:* `cg_preproc.*` (a real C preprocessor: `#if/#elif/defined`, function-like macros, `##`, `#error`), `cg_to_glsl.*` (single-pass, type-directed Cg -> GLSL 3.30 translator: implicit conversions, overloads, in/out/inout, swizzles, matrix swizzles, structs with semantics, Cg's leaky `for (int i ...)` scope, mul/lerp/frac/saturate/tex2D/ddx/fmod/modf ...; `asm` and anything unknown is an error), `cg_program.*` (GL compile and link, programs cached in memory by define list, identical GLSL texts share shader objects). **Matrix convention:** a Cg `floatRxC` matrix is a GLSL `matRxC` holding the transpose; uniform data uploads verbatim as the PS3 constant registers (`glUniformMatrix4fv(..., GL_FALSE, rows)`), `mul(M, v)` is `v * S`, `mul(v, M)` is `S * v`, `mul(A, B)` is `S_B * S_A`, `M[i]` is `S[i]`, `M._mRC` is `S[R][C]`; `cg_translate_test` checks ten cases with a known matrix on the GPU (also a `float3x4` array element, `(float3x3)M`, `float3x3(a, b, c)`, `mul(M, M)`). Attributes sit at the NV40 slots by semantic, varyings are `v_<member>`, pixel outputs `o_<member>`, samplers' texture units are their `register(s<N>)`. *Game side* (`k2000/render`): `ShaderVariantKey.h/.cpp` (key struct 0x14 bytes; `BuildForMesh_maybe` 0xf1508 with the thunks 0xf1c90/0xf1c98; `Equals`, `MatchesVertexShader_maybe`, `MatchesPixelShader_maybe`, `Init`; `ShaderGen_CollectExternFragments_maybe` 0xf48c0 with its material-mode jump table read from the assembly, `CollectVertexUniforms_maybe` 0xf44c0, `CollectPixelUniforms_maybe` 0xf41b0, `GetShaderModeForKey_maybe` 0xf8840; `ShaderVariant_GetProgram(key)` returns the linked GL program), `MeshShaderPassState.h/.cpp` (`ConstructMeshShaderPassState` 0xefe08 and all getters BuildForMesh and `ComputePassCount_maybe` 0xefc08 call, `MaterialMode_GetMaxShaderSlotCount_maybe` 0xf7eb8, the slot allocators). The scene inputs the original reads from the light manager, render states, shadow service and the shader manager's flag word (+0x38 pixel lighting, +0x3c mode 6, +0x8c flags) are `ShaderSceneInputs` (all zero until steps 5-7: STUB(C2.4 step 5/6/7)); `CLightManager_ComputeLightCount_maybe` 0x9b208 and `GatherLightsTouchingSphere_maybe` 0x9c150 are step 6. *Exit* (`shader_variants_test`, hidden window, AMD GL 3.3): the level's own materials (LVL010 + the start sector STR01002: 3,663 geometries with an atomic, 2,912 meshes, 55 skinned; modes 0:453, 2:138, 5:169, 12:2147, 14:5) give **49 distinct keys = 22 distinct vertex GLSL programs and 8 pixel programs in 23 linked pairs** (by the original's own `MatchesVertexShader/MatchesPixelShader` comparison: 23 and 10); all translate, compile and link with 0 errors. A sweep of every key over 0/2 directional and point lights, fog + shadow and the special light adds 503 more define lists (159 vertex / 61 pixel programs, 175 pairs in total), also 0 errors. Finding: the shader manager's pixel-lighting mode (`externPixelLightning`, +0x38, default 0) cannot apply to materials without a normal map: the Cg reads `f3NormalLS`, which only the normal-output effects declare (the original's `cgc` would fail the same way), so the sweep restricts it to mode 12 with its aux texture. Not ported: `ShaderVariantList_*` 0xf0f00 / 0xf26a0 / 0xf2900 (the offline list of every key per material mode, for pre-generating `shfct.bin`), `BuildFromPassState_maybe` 0xf0108 and `BuildForImmediate3D_maybe` 0xf07a8 (post-effect and immediate passes, step 8), the uniform fillers `MeshShaderPassState_Fill*` and `FillSkinBoneMatrixPaletteUniform` (step 3).

Quirk kept: `ComputeLightCount` counts a light only when an odd number of its (r, g, b, 1 - a) lanes reach 1/255 (the AltiVec code ANDs the lane sum with 1). The point lights stay off: `CKPointLightNodeFx` Start / Update (the code that enables and animates them) is started by the fx service (not ported: its own checkpoint). *Fog:* `ApplySceneRenderStatesAndFog` 0x9ed38 for real (density by camera: main / far; fog state on when the camera's fog plane is not beyond its far plane; the colours `FogManager_GetMainColor` / `GetColorForFarPass`), the camera service's fog accessors (`CKSrvCamera::GetFogStart/End/MainFogDensity/...`, the blended values of a camera transition read at +0xbf0..+0xc3c), `IsFogEnabled` = fog state and the level's fog texture, the fog texture (`CKGraphic +0x1c`, the level's `fog_l060`, a 256x16 DXT5 ramp) bound on unit 7 with clamp / filter 5 (the blank texture when fog is off), `RwRenderStateSet(FOGDENSITY)` takes a float pointer (the rwcore port took the bits). LVL010's camera fog: colour black, start 5, end 200, density 0.0032. *Sky:* the backgrounds of the sectors were never shown: `CRenderer_PostLoad` 0xa2180 (every background hidden, the video manager first in the renderable list; called by the new `CKGraphic_PostLoad` 0x5a530), `CBackgroundManager_PreUpdate` 0x946e0 (the active sectors' root nodes shown; `CKLevel_IterateActiveSectors` 0x2f150), `CanBeUpdated` of the light and background managers; the far group now draws 4 background geometries (the cavern's far walls: the level has no bright sky). `SyncSceneCamerasAndClipPlanes` takes the far-texture object (0x4d0d) into the near plane (inactive until step 8). `AdvanceToNextPass` 0xf55a8 was checked against the assembly: identical. *Gap closed:* `MatFXUVAnim_UpdateMaterial_maybe` 0xbd228 (the five UV effect types for modes 5, 6, 12, 14: scroll with limits, rotation, flip-book, shear and circular wobble; a delegated port with its own test `matfx_uvanim_test`; `Mat4_RotateInPlace` 0x240498) is called from `CGeometry::EnqueueVisible`. Tests: `light_rig_test` (synthetic lights, the odd-lane quirk, colour curve), both presets 31/31 (one x64 `level_load_test` crash in LVL120 seen once and not reproducible in six more runs, with or without cdb); gates OK. Exit by eye (`work/scratch-c24/shots/newgame_300.png`, not committed): the courtyard now fades into the black fog with distance; RPCS3 comparison pending.

Port each from the original or move it to its real checkpoint with the reason.

#### Rules
Those of C2.2 apply. In addition: the Cg source and `shfct.bin` are game data: read them from the game folder at run time, never copy them (or GLSL generated from them) into the repository; GLSL written by hand for an override is a port of one function, like ported C++, and carries the source location in a comment.

### C2.5 Animated and skinned characters

**Goal.** `dotd.exe` draws LVL010's characters (the hero, Cynder, the enemies, the animated props) as the original does: skinned geometry on the GPU through the uber-shader's skinning path, posed every frame by the game's own animation system. That system is the controllers (`CKBlenderController` over `CKAnmKeyFrameController`), the blend slots and crossfades, and RenderWare's RtAnim interpolator with RpHAnim key frames. The opening in-engine scene (#13, C2.3) plays its actor animations, and the hero idles at control. *Which* animation the hero or an enemy picks (movement states, attacks) is C2.6 / C2.7. Here every caller that exists today (cinematic blocs, `CKHkS08Hero` init, the crumbly zones, the enemies' idle) gets real animation underneath. Stubbed callers stay stubbed until their checkpoint.

**Work list (generated):** `work/c25-packs/anim.tsv` (`python work/c25_pack.py`, needs Ghidra; same columns as C2.4's). It holds the roots by area and everything they reach in the static call graph up to depth 4. Measured 2026-10-25:

| Area | Functions | Ported | Ran in the timeline |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| node | 33 | 12 | 13 |
| ctrl | 16 | 0 | 3 |
| rtanim | 14 | 0 | 0 |
| hanim | 10 | 0 | 0 |
| skin | 114 | 1 | 5 |
| team | 68 | 2 | 5 |

The tracer only hooks game code, so the RenderWare rows (rtanim, hanim, most of skin and team) never show as ran even though they do. The call graph misses virtual calls (the controllers' vtables: `Evaluate` and `UpdateActiveState` are vfXX slots, so follow them from the vtables 0xb16590 / 0xb16608), the RW plugin callbacks (RpSkin and RpHAnim register stream and constructor callbacks at plugin attach) and the skin pipeline's instance callbacks.

**What the data is (`python work/c25_anim_scan.py LVL010`, USRDIR in `DOTD_USRDIR`):**
- **Animations (chunk 0x1b):**
  - `LVL010.KP3` has 422 (633,801 key frames, 500 s in all).
  - The cinematic streams `STR01000..01003.KP3` have 113 / 97 / 99 / 89.
  - All of them are **version 0x101** with interpolation scheme 1 (RpHAnim standard key frames, 36 bytes: the previous frame link, time, quaternion, translation) and flags 0.
  - Version 0x101 is the PS3 build's. RW 3.7's headers say 0x100. 0x101 has a second key frame count after the first (0 in every LVL010 animation), as `RtAnimAnimationStreamRead` 0x707d58 shows. **Port this reader from the assembly, not from the RW source.**
- **Skins (chunk 0x116, inside the geometry extensions):** 19, listed as bones / max weights per vertex: 2/1, 3/2 ×2, 6/1, 15/1 ×2, 20/3, 45/1, 45/2, 45/4 ×2, 54/3, 60/1 ×4, 60/3 ×3. The 60-bone ones are the dragons (`(nodes, kfsize)` below), and the 45-bone ones are the hero-sized humanoids and trolls. *Check this against the objects that own them: it is an inference.*
- **HAnim hierarchies (0x11e with nodes, read since C2.1):** 23, listed as nodes / key frame size: 2/36, 3/36 ×17, 15/36, 45/36, 60/36 ×3.
- C2.4 step 1 already reads the 19 skin chunks as bytes (`RpGeometry::skinData`) and the native vertex data of the skinned geometries.

**What the original does (addresses for the steps below):**
- **The animations' storage:**
  - `CSectorAnimation_LoadEntry_maybe` 0x79898 (ported as far as the shim, `Renderware/rtanim/RtAnim.h`: the 0x1b payload is kept as a blob) and `CAnimationDictionary` (key 0x49: handle = sector << 24 | index, `CAnimationDictionary_PostLoad` 0x7a288).
  - The stream animations `CKStreamAnimCinematicBloc` / `BeginBloc` / `EndBloc` (the STR files).
- **RtAnim** (library, TOC2):
  - Reading: `RtAnimAnimationStreamRead` 0x707d58 / `RtAnimAnimationRead` 0x708080 / `RtAnimAnimationCreate` 0x708118, `RtAnimAnimationDestroy_maybe` 0x706da0, `RtAnimAnimationGetNumNodes_maybe` 0x706e30.
  - Interpolator: `RtAnimInterpolatorCreate` 0x706e90, `SetCurrentAnim` 0x706fa8, `AddAnimTime` 0x708298, `SubAnimTime_maybe` 0x708870, `SetCurrentTime` 0x708c70, the callbacks 0x707188 / 0x707198.
  - Blending and sub-interpolators: `_BlendAllNodes_maybe` 0x7071a8, `_AddAllNodes_maybe` 0x7072a0, `CreateSubInterpolator_maybe` 0x707c50, `AddSubInterpolator_maybe` 0x707388, `BlendSubInterpolator_maybe` 0x7074b0, `_CopyNodeFrames_maybe` 0x707820.

Also the compressed scheme `RtCompressedKeyFrameInterpolate_maybe` 0x71fdf0 (registered, unused by LVL010).

  - Its 0x24-byte struct is in `Renderware/rphanim/RpHAnim.h` (`currentAnim` is null today).
- **The game's animation layer:**

#### Tools
The C2.2 / C2.4 tool lists apply (build, `newgame_test`, cdb, Ghidra, XXL2, gates, `DOTD_SCREENSHOT`, `DOTD_CAMERA_FROM`, `frame_compare.py`, STYLE). Plus:
- **RenderWare 3.7.0.2 source**, read-only. The `rphanim`, `rtanim`, `rpskin` and `rpteam` plugins have **headers only** in `misc/inc/` (`rphanim.h`, `rtanim.h`, `rpskin.h`, `rpteam.h`), with no `.c` for these plugins. The headers give the struct layouts, constants and API meaning, so the code is ported from the PS3 assembly. Keep the RW names where the header names the function.
- **XXL2 symbols** for the game side: `python work/xxl2_syms.py find CAnimatedNode` (and `CKAnimationController`, `CSkinGeometry`, `CCloneManager`, `CKAnimationMarkContainer`). Member names for the `m_unkXX` fields. Check DotD's code where the layout differs (the CAnimatedNode header says it does after the hierarchy).
- **Live bones:** `work/gmem.py` reads a node's matrices in RPCS3 (`u32(node + 0x2c)` = hierarchy, `u32(hier + 8)` = the matrix array, 0x40 per bone, big-endian floats). Step 4 extends `rpcs3_frame_capture.py` to save them at held frames.

#### Step 1. Read the animations and build the hierarchies
1. Replace the shim `RwShim_RtAnimAnimationStreamRead` (`Renderware/rtanim/RtAnim.*`) with the real `RtAnimAnimation`: the PS3 0x101 reader 0x707d58 (scheme lookup by type id in the registered scheme table, the two counts, flags, duration, the allocation of count × scheme key frame size + count2 × the second size + 0x1c), and the scheme's `StreamRead` (`RpHAnimStdKeyFrameStreamRead` 0x6e88c0: the previous-frame links are stored as indices and become pointers). Register the RpHAnim standard scheme the way `RtAnimInitialize` 0x707870 / the RpHAnim plugin open `_rpHAnimOpen_maybe` 0x6e8070 do. Keep the 0x24 bytes `CSectorAnimation_LoadEntry` reads (`RtAnimSectorEntryData`) where the PS3 keeps them, after the key frames.
2. The users of the blob (`anim->streamed`, grep `streamed` in `rtanim` and `CKAnmKeyFrameController::GetDurationOf`) move to the real fields. `GetDuration` reads `RtAnimAnimation::duration`.
3. `RpHAnimHierarchy` for real: `RpHAnimHierarchyCreate` 0x6e7510 / `CreateFromHierarchy` 0x6e7840 / `Attach` 0x6e8170 / `AttachFrameIndex` 0x6e8248 / `Detach` (the matrix array 16-byte aligned, `pFrame` per node). `CAnimatedNode_SetHAnimHierarchy_maybe` 0xc9a08, `CSkinGeometry_SetHAnimHierarchy_maybe` 0x8b368 and `CAnimatedNode_PostLoad` 0xc9d48 as far as the hierarchy goes.
4. The stream animations (STR files) load through the same reader. Check what `CKStreamAnimCinematicBeginBloc` reads (`grep -rn STUB dotd/src/k2000/cinematics/CKStreamAnim*`).
5. **Exit:** a new test `tests/c15/anim_read_test.cpp` loads LVL010 like `level_load_test` and reports what it read: 422 animations with 633,801 key frames and 500.0 s in all, every one version 0x101 / scheme 1. It also opens the four STR files: 113 / 97 / 99 / 89 animations, the totals `c25_anim_scan.py` prints. Every key frame's previous link points backwards inside its animation. Every CAnimatedNode with a hierarchy has an attached frame per node. `level_load_test` still shows 0 mismatches over all levels (end offsets unchanged), the gates are OK, and both presets are green.

**Status step 1 (2026-10-25): done** (report `work/c25-report-step1.json`). `RtAnimAnimation` is real (PS3 0x1c-byte header: scheme, `numFrames`, `numFrames2`, flags, `duration`, `pFrames`, `customData`; key frames behind it, then the custom data whose last 0x24 bytes are `RtAnimSectorEntryData`), read by `RtAnimAnimationStreamRead` 0x707d58 and the registered scheme table (`RtAnimInitialize`, `RtAnimRegisterInterpolationScheme`, `RtAnimGetInterpolatorInfo`; scheme 1 by `RpHAnimPluginOpen` = `_rpHAnimOpen_maybe`, scheme 2 by `RtCompressedKeyFrameRegister`, both registered in `CKGraphic::InitializeModule` before `CAnimationManager`'s constructor adds 0x24 to their custom data size). Key frames are `RpHAnimStdKeyFrame` with real `prevFrame` pointers. `RpHAnimHierarchy` Create / CreateFromHierarchy / Attach / AttachFrameIndex / Detach / FrameSetHierarchy / IDGetIndex / the frame copy callback are ported; `CAnimatedNode::SetHAnimHierarchy`, `CSkinGeometry_SetHAnimHierarchy`, `RpSkinAtomicSetHAnimHierarchy` (`RpAtomic::skinHierarchy`) and the hierarchy part of `CAnimatedNode::PostLoad` too. The STR sector files go through the same reader. `tests/c15/anim_read_test.cpp` (new): LVL010.KP3 422 animations / 633,801 key frames / 500.0 s, STR01000..01003 113 / 97 / 99 / 89 (158,681 / 141,446 / 152,431 / 100,118 key frames), all scheme 1, flags 0, no second array, layout as the PS3's; 46 CAnimatedNodes with hierarchies (823 nodes) on their root frames. Two facts that differ from this plan: (1) a node's **first** key frame links *forward* to the node's last (wrap for looping), every other link goes back (the test classifies 608,939 + 24,862 links in LVL010, 0 bad); (2) the level's hierarchies have **no bone frames** (the bone frame list is the root frame alone, HAnim id -1) and nothing in the load path calls `RpHAnimHierarchyAttach` (no caller in the game), so `pFrame` stays null in the original too; Attach is tested on a synthetic tree. **Seams for step 2:** the scheme records hold null for the apply / interpolate / blend / add / mulrecip callbacks (`RpHAnim.cpp`, `RpHAnimPluginOpen`); `RtAnimInterpolatorCreate` (`RtAnim.cpp`) returns null and `RpHAnimHierarchy::currentAnim` is `RtAnimInterpolator*` (forward declared); `RpHAnimHierarchyUpdateMatrices` is not ported. **Step 3 note:** the original's animation word (dictionary entries after `CAnimationDictionary_PostLoad` 0x7a288, `CAnimatedNode` +0x5c, controller +0x58) is the `RtAnimAnimation*` (duration read at +0x10, `Anim_GetDuration` 0x7ab60); dotd still keeps 32-bit dictionary handles (the x64 build cannot store a pointer in those words), and `CKAnmKeyFrameController::GetDurationOf` resolves them through the manager. `CAnimatedNode_PostLoad`'s first-animation part is a `STUB(C2.5 step 3)`.

#### Step 2. The interpolator and the key frame scheme
1. Port RtAnim's interpolator: `RtAnimInterpolatorCreate` 0x706e90, `Destroy`, `SetCurrentAnim` 0x706fa8 (the initial key frames per node), `AddAnimTime` 0x708298 (advance, loop with the callback, the key frame walk), `SubAnimTime` 0x708870, `SetCurrentTime` 0x708c70, the callbacks. Also the sub-interpolators and blends (`CreateSubInterpolator` 0x707c50, `AddSubInterpolator` 0x707388, `BlendSubInterpolator` 0x7074b0, `_BlendAllNodes` 0x7071a8, `_AddAllNodes` 0x7072a0, `_CopyNodeFrames` 0x707820).
2. The scheme callbacks: `RpHAnimKeyFrameInterpolate` 0x6e8e48 (the quaternion slerp/nlerp and translation lerp *exactly* as the PS3 code computes them: same operation order, same float precision, same branch for near-equal quaternions), `RpHAnimKeyFrameBlend` 0x6e89c0, `Add` 0x6e86c8, `MulRecip` 0x6e85d8, `MakeDelta` 0x708cd0.
3. `RpHAnimHierarchyUpdateMatrices` 0x6e63b8: interpolated key frames to the matrix array, through the push/pop parent-matrix stack of the node flags. Local or global per the hierarchy flags.
4. **Exit:** a unit test (`tests/c15/anim_interp_test.cpp`) takes one LVL010 animation (pick a 60-node dragon one and a 3-node prop one), plays it at 1/60 s steps for 600 frames on a hierarchy and checks:
   - the time wraps at the duration;
   - the key frame index walk never goes backwards except at a loop;
   - the matrices stay orthonormal (|det − 1| < 1e-4);
   - the matrices at t = 0 equal the first key frames.

   The comparison with the original is step 4.

**Status step 2 (2026-10-25): done** (report `work/c25-report-step23.json`). `RtAnimInterpolator` is the PS3's 0x50 bytes (RW 3.7's plus a second key frame size at +0x2c; the walk steps by the first or second size as the key frame time's lowest bit says; the interpolate callback gets the node index as a sixth argument). Ported: Create / Destroy / SetCurrentAnim / AddAnimTime / SubAnimTime / SetCurrentTime / the two callback setters / SetKeyFrameCallBacks 0x707998 / CreateSubInterpolator / Blend / AddTogether / Copy / `RtAnimAnimationGetNumNodes` / `RtAnimAnimationDestroy`, the scheme 1 callbacks (apply 0x6e84d8 = `RpHAnimKeyFrameApply`, interpolate, blend: the slerp with Dinkumware's acosf / sinf inlined branch for branch, the lerp at dot >= 0.999, the in-place negation of the second key frame; add and mulrecip in the original's load/store order) and `RpHAnimHierarchyUpdateMatrices` (local space, global and sub-hierarchy roots, the push/pop parent stack, frame modelling / LTM writes; `STUB(C2.5)` for the NOMATRICES variant, which no LVL010 hierarchy has: all are flag 0x4000). Not ported, no caller in the game (only RW's own function table): `AddSubInterpolator` 0x707388, `BlendSubInterpolator` 0x7074b0, `MakeDelta` 0x708cd0. x64: interpolated frames are `RtAnimHostInterpKeyFrameSize(ps3 size)` bytes (the two header pointers). `tests/c15/anim_interp_test.cpp`: a 60-node (1.75 s, 1283 key frames) and a 3-node animation (2 s) on level hierarchies, 600 steps of 1/60 s: 5 / 4 loops, time and walk exact, t = 0 equals the first key frames. **The plan's |det − 1| < 1e-4 does not hold in the original's math**: RW's lerp branch (dot >= 0.999) does not renormalise, so a lerped local matrix is s²R + (1 − s²)I with |q|² down to 0.9995 (|det − 1| up to 1.9e-3 per node, 3.5e-3 down a 60-node chain); the test checks slerped nodes at 1e-4, lerped ones against 4(1 − |q|²), and every matrix's det against its local det times its parent's (1.6e-7).

Animation time comes from the game's frame time (the same dt the rest of the loop uses), never from the wall clock.

Run it at `video_end+1`, `video_end+120` and `control`. Every node with a controller has an animation playing and the time advances by the frame's dt. The gates stay OK (the gate's object fields that animation writes, if any, now match or get a named rule).

#### Step 4. Compare the poses with the original
1. Extend `work/rpcs3_frame_capture.py` with `--bones`: at each held frame (render phase, the same moment the cameras are read) it walks the same CAnimatedNode set and saves the matrices in the `DOTD_BONES_OUT` format. Find the nodes live the way `live_objects.py` enumerates objects (class key of CAnimatedNode, `kp3`/census ids so both sides name a node the same way).
2. `work/bones_compare.py dotd.json rpcs3.json`: per node and bone, the largest translation difference and rotation angle difference. Sort by node.
3. **Exit:** at `video_end+1` and `video_end+120` (scene #13's actors playing their cinematic animations) and at `control` (the hero's idle):
   - each node's bones are within 1e-3 world units / 0.1° of the original;
   - the nodes left over are listed with their reason (a controller whose animation choice is C2.6 / C2.7 logic, for example).

   Float differences that grow over time mean the dt or the interpolation order differs: find which before moving on.

**Status step 4 (2026-10-25): done** (report `work/c25-report-step4.json`). *Determinism:* two native runs differed because the level load (mode 1) took 70 or 75 frames depending on the load worker's speed; the harness clock is the frame count, so every later absolute time and the float frame dt (`IKTime_Update` 0x53fb0 subtracts two float absolute times) moved by an ulp (controller times 4e-6 apart). Fix (platform, off by default): `dotd::platform::SetDeterministicRequests` (sys_thread.h), on in `c15::BootToFrontEnd`; a poll (`KRequest_IsFinished` 0x57978) of a queued or running request of the load queue (`CKLoadRequestQueue`, `keCThreadPool::SetWaitOnPoll`; not the memcard queue, whose work waits on the main thread) waits for it. Five runs are byte-identical. *Tools:* `rpcs3_frame_capture.py --bones` (DIR/bones.json after each target's Present, same ids / CRC / controller words, plus the frame clock), `work/bones_compare.py` (per node and bone translation / angle; `--pose-spec` and `--retimed`), and `newgame_test` `DOTD_BONES_POSE_FROM` (the nodes RPCS3 posed, posed again with the original's animation, time and blend weights: moment `retimed:<name>`). *Result:* frame-aligned poses differ (up to 0.5 units / 12° on the actors) **because the original's animation time is real time**: RPCS3's dt is the measured frame time (0.0164-0.0165 s here) and a capture hold makes the next frame's dt 0.1 s (the clamp), so its actors are at 0.0608 / 2.0855 s (skipped) and 2.0579 s (watched +120) against dotd's 0.0167 / 2.0000 s; the dt, not the interpolation order. With the original's times (retimed), every node posed on both sides matches to 7e-9 units and 0.0000° at +1, +120 and control, skipped and watched (heroes, troll, actors, the 3-node 0x0038054b), i.e. the RtAnim / RpHAnim / controller / blend port is exact. *Leftovers:* heroes 0x0006054b / 0x0018054b at t = 0 of their first animation (C2.6: the hero update; RPCS3 plays 0x23 / 0xde and at control blends 0x25 + 0x23, 0xe0 / 0xde + 0xde); 0x0038054b (3 nodes, at (0.10, 30.0, 428.0) by the heroes) has animation word 0x01000000 at t = 0 in the original at control and none in dotd (setter not identified; with the hero / control code, C2.6); visibility differences: the actor 0x003c054b is posed in dotd only at +1 / +120 with the same capture camera (its animation time differs by the dt; whether the frustum test or its sphere differs is not settled: check in step 5 when it is drawn); at control dotd poses the troll and the crowd with the post-Present camera, RPCS3 did not: the control frame's render camera is not captured). Watched +1 shows the video's last image: nothing posed on either side.

#### Rules
Those of C2.2 and C2.4 apply. In addition:

### C2.6 The hero under control (movement, camera, collision)

**Goal.** After control is given in LVL010, `dotd.exe` lets the player walk, run and jump with Spyro, and turn the camera, as the original does:
- the partner (Cynder, chained to Spyro) follows under AI;
- the heroes idle and move with the original's animations;
- the ground and walls hold them;
- the game camera follows them;
- flying and climbing work where LVL010 asks for them.

The proof is a **replay**: input and frame times recorded in RPCS3, fed to `dotd`, give the same hero positions, states and camera, frame by frame.

**Out of scope:**
- Combat: combos, attacks, being hurt, death, fury, abilities and enemies are C2.7. The hero's combat states stay stubs here, with a log line.
- Level scripting beyond what walking touches: doors, triggers, pickups, sequences and air currents as mechanisms are C2.8.

**What C2.5 left for this checkpoint** (from its status lines; check them in the C2.5 section):
- The heroes stand at t = 0 of their first animation. RPCS3 plays 0x23 / 0xde and at control blends 0x25 + 0x23 and 0xe0 / 0xde + 0xde: the hero update chooses them.
- The 3-node object 0x0038054b by the heroes has animation word 0x01000000 at control in the original and none in dotd. Its setter is not found yet; look in the hero / control code.
- The main camera does not follow the cinematic or the heroes (`CKSrvCamera_UpdateFrame` 0x137ae0), so dotd frames still need `DOTD_CAMERA_FROM`.
- The heroes face another way than in RPCS3 at control (their node frames).
- The actor 0x003c054b keeps the file's y 34.9 where RPCS3 has 34.531, probably a ground snap: check it in step 3.
- The original's animation clock is **real time**: RPCS3's dt is the measured frame time, about 0.0164–0.0165 s, clamped at 0.1 s. That is why step 1 builds the replay on recorded frame times.

#### Tools
The C2.2 / C2.4 / C2.5 tool lists apply: build, `newgame_test` and its `DOTD_*` options, cdb, Ghidra, XXL2 (`python work/xxl2_syms.py find <class>` for member and slot names of the engine-side classes: camera, collision, input, physics; for the hero classes check with `find` first: DotD's hero is Spyro-specific and XXL2's hero code differs), the gates, STYLE, `rpcs3_frame_capture.py`, `bones_compare.py`, `frame_compare.py`. Reference documents: `outputs/ability-analysis/command-path.md` (input → hero commands), `outputs/ability-system.md` (the hero's state flow), `outputs/binary-naming.md` (the per-family vtable slot tables).

#### Step 1. The replay harness (record in RPCS3, replay in dotd, compare)
Everything after this step is checked with it, so build it first.
1. **Recorder:** `work/rpcs3_replay_record.py`, built on `rpcs3_frame_capture.py`'s boot.
   - **Boot:** it starts RPCS3 with the mod patch off and drives New game with `--skip-video`.
   - **Sampling:** from the first frame of the video skip it reads, at every frame, right after the render counter (u16 0xc29694) changes. At that moment the frame's update has finished and the next one has not started. Check this against the main loop order (`CKGameLoop` → update → `RenderGameFrame` 0xa1c08 → Present) and pick the read point that gives one consistent frame.
   - **What it reads per frame** (a line of JSON, or a binary record with a header):
     - the frame number;
     - the game's clock: the value `KGetExactTime` returned for this frame and the scalable game dt of `IKTime` (`m_afDeltaTime`);
     - the raw pad data the game got for port 0: the `CellPadData` copy `PS3PadDevice` keeps (find it in `PollPs3ControllerState`, vt+0x2c of `k2000/input/PS3PadDevice.cpp`; reach the device from the engine singleton u32(0xc50f20) → the CKInput module, slot map in `outputs/binary-naming.md`);
     - the `rand` state;
     - the state to compare, below.
   - **Missed frames:** it stops with an error if a frame is missed. Python can follow 60 fps by busy-polling like the capture script. If it can't, lower RPCS3's frame limit for the recording: the replay uses the recorded times, so the speed RPCS3 ran at does not matter.
   - **The user plays:** at control it prints "play now" and records `--frames N` frames, by default 1800 (30 s at 60 fps).
2. **State to compare, per hero (both):**
   - the node's LTM (position and axes);
   - velocity, if the hero keeps one (find it in the dynamics);
   - movement mode +0x1808, flags +0x358;
   - the current state id of each of the four machines (find where `IKHkHero` keeps them);
   - the current animation word and time of the inner blender.

   Also per frame: the main camera's frame LTM, view window and near/far (as `rpcs3_frame_capture.py` reads them), the GameManager state +0x2f5, the active sector and the focused dragon index.
3. **Player:** `newgame_test` option `DOTD_REPLAY=<file>` (platform debug code, off by default).
   - It boots with `DOTD_NEWGAME_SKIP=1`.
   - From the video's end (frame numbering = `rpcs3_frame_capture.py`'s `video_end`) it feeds, per frame:
     - the recorded exact time to `KGetExactTime` (`k2000/system/CKTime.cpp`: a platform time source, like the pad's);
     - the recorded pad words through `platform::SetPadSource` (`platform/cell/pad.h`: made for replays).
   - It sets the `rand` state at `video_end` from the recording.
   - It writes the same per-frame state (`DOTD_REPLAY_OUT=<file>`).
4. **Compare:** `work/replay_compare.py rpcs3.rec dotd.rec`. It reports the first frame and field that differ beyond the tolerance (positions 1e-4 units, angles 0.01°, states and words exact), then a per-field summary.
   - **Expected float limits:** RPCS3 fuses multiply-adds like the PS3 (`DFMA` on), so the port writes every original `fmadds` / `fmsubs` / `fnmadds` / `fnmsubs` with `ppc::fmadds` etc. (STYLE.md; the compilers do not contract on their own: `/fp:precise`, `-ffp-contract=off`). The VMX denormal flush is not reproduced (BLUEPRINT §7).
   - **First divergences:** on each one, find the function: the tracer's call counts, cdb, the original's assembly.
5. **Exit:**
   - an *idle* recording (the user presses nothing for 10 s after control) replays with the same dt sequence as the recording (the player checks it);
   - the actors' and the troll's animation times equal RPCS3's at every frame (C2.5's frame-aligned differences disappear, because the dt is the original's now);
   - `replay_compare.py` reports the heroes' fields as the only differences, since the hero is not ported yet.

**Status step 1 (2026-10-25): done** (report `work/c26-report-step1.json`). *Tools:* `python work/rpcs3_replay_record.py --skip-video [--frames N] --out DIR` → `DIR/rpcs3.rec` (JSON lines `dotd-replay-1`; fields listed in `dotd/tests/c15/newgame_replay.inc`); `newgame_test` with `DOTD_REPLAY=DIR/rpcs3.rec DOTD_REPLAY_OUT=dotd.rec` (implies the skip); `python work/replay_compare.py rpcs3.rec dotd.rec [--shown] [--json OUT]`. *Read point:* clock (IKTime) and pad device data (+0x118) when the frame counter changes (both final after the update, untouchable by the next frame), the rest in a hold of the **main PPU thread only** right after `PresentPSGLDeviceBuffers` (the frame's post-state, as `newgame_test` after a Step); checks: counter +1 per frame (missed frame = error), clock unchanged between the two reads (torn frame = error in gameplay). 60 fps without touching RPCS3's frame limit (hold 1.3 ms mean). The libc rand state is the main PPU thread's TLS block (RPCS3: first slot of the 0x40000 TLS area, the PT_TLS image's second word 0x00aea7d0 finds it; 0x10000060 here). *Third real-time input:* the opening scene's sekens line ends when the speech stream's RWA player is assigned and released (`CKSekens_UpdateCurrentLine` 0x1f6410: `HasPlayer` = CKStreamObject +0x0c, `IsFinished` FUN_0011a538), real time in RPCS3 (today control at +571..573 with the video skipped, also with `rpcs3_frame_capture.py`; +557 earlier); the recorder reads it, the player feeds it through `speech_model::SetReplaySource` (the state after frame N-1 answers frame N; a position from the frame the sekens saw one, flag 0x800). *Idle recording* (`work/scratch-c26/rec/idle`, control +571, 600 frames after it): the fed clock (exact time, unscaled / system dt, state) equals the recording bit for bit in all 1171 frames, x86 and x64 (their outputs are identical); the scaled dt equals it up to +665, where the original's time scale ramps to 0 (+665..+694: the first fight's slow motion after the partner enters AICombat at +641, C2.7); control at +571 as recorded; the troll's and the six actors' animations (word and time t of every weighted controller) equal RPCS3's bit for bit in every frame +1..+1171 except at their animation switches, where the original crossfades (the node was drawn the frame before: `KBase98D_vf50` finds a pose) and dotd cuts (not drawn: the main camera does not follow the scene's cameras, step 5); same times, weights differ for 14-30 frames. *Other differences, by owner:* hero fields from +0 (states 255, facing, y 29.9 vs 30.001, animations at t = 0; steps 2-4); camera (step 5) and what depends on the drawn set (posed, evaluated "anm"/"time", crossfade weights, controller flags); rand (the original draws ~50 numbers per frame: FX, particles, sound; dotd almost none); what built up before video_end (the actors' inactive first controllers' words / t from the level start, node 0x0000054b's word 0) — the replay starts at video_end; node 0x0004054b (3 nodes at (16, 39, 380)) plays 0x0a from +1 and 0x0038054b (by the heroes) 0x01000000 (setters not found); scene #16 (starts at control +1 on both sides) gives its four actors 0x0054..0x005a054b animation 0x0100002f in the original only, and its elements part at +683 (after the fight's slow motion). An idle recording reaches the first fight's start ~70 frames after control: hero-only comparisons of later steps should end at +640 or include the fight's intro.

#### Step 2. The hero group, the state machine dispatch and standing still
1. **The dispatch.** Replace the descriptor words in the state rows with native handlers. Keep the rows' PS3 layout for the fields game code reads (the state id byte, the order), and add a side table that maps the descriptor address (the value the original row holds) to a C++ pointer to member of `CKHkS08Hero`, filled once at start-up.
   - Port the four update functions in the original's order (`UpdateAnimStateMachine_maybe` 0x352468, `UpdateActionStateMachine_maybe` 0x3656b0, the control update, `UpdateGroundLocomotionState_maybe` 0x31a040 for the locomotion machine): the enter / exit / condition calls, the clock argument and the 0xff "no state".
   - A handler that is not ported yet logs once (`DOTD_STUB_ONCE` with its name and checkpoint) and does nothing, so a replay shows where it was needed.
   - The same mechanism replaces the descriptor words of `CKHkS08Weapon.cpp:72`, `CKHkS08TransportableKey.cpp:133`, `CKHkS08ElectricGauge.cpp:119` and `CKSrvDynamic.h:34`, and their handlers are ported with their checkpoints (C2.7 / C2.8).
2. **The hero group:** `IKGrpHero_Update` 0x2c8870, `CKGrpS08Hero_SetFocusedDragon` 0x2cc3f0 (then the GameManager's focused-hero getters `CKS08GameManager_2.cpp:470` / `_3.cpp:571` work), and the pair updates listed above. Which hero is the player's and which the AI's is the control machine's state: Player 5 / PlayerFollowsFocus 7 against AIFollowGround 8.
3. **`UpdateMovement` vt+0x17c 0x2dc730 and its per-frame calls**, as far as standing needs them: ground surface flags, mode flags and tether timers (`CKHkS08HeroCameraTarget.cpp:76`'s carrier hero stays a stub: no carrier at the start), regen / element timeout, armor, footstep sounds, the shadow ground point, `vfF4`.
4. **The standing states:** control Inactive / Player / PlayerFollowsFocus / ControlLocked / ScriptedMoveTo / AIFollowGround (and the catch-up states 100 / 101 the AI uses when it falls behind), animation LocomotionGround with its `GroundAnimState` sub-machine, locomotion Ground, action FreeMovement. Their conditions. The animations they pick go through the C2.5 API (`CKHkS08Hero_PlayAnimOnInnerBlender_maybe`, the blend slots).
5. **The C2.5 leftovers here:** the heroes' first animations and blends at control, the 3-node object 0x0038054b's animation word, the heroes' facing (their node frames at control).
6. **`CKHkSequenceMgr_StopCurrentSequence_maybe` 0x463690 / `DetachHeroes_maybe`** (`CKHkSequenceMgr.cpp:359`): the heroes' part is here. The rest goes to C2.8 if it is scripting.
7. **Exit:**
   - the idle replay matches RPCS3 for every hero field, including the four state ids, the animation words and times;
   - bones at control and at control + 300 match (`bones_compare.py`, with `DOTD_BONES_OUT` in the replay run);
   - the heroes face the right way in a `DOTD_CAMERA_FROM` frame at control.

1. `CKSrvCamera_UpdateFrame` 0x137ae0 (the `UpdateCKSrvCamera` stub) and the service functions listed above, `IKCamera_UpdateTrackBase` / `CKCamera_Update`, the camera tracks the stub in `IKCamera.h:23` skips (`CKCameraClassicTrack3` first; the other track classes as LVL010's census has them), the sector cameras, the camera volumes (`CameraVolume_ContainsPoint_maybe` 0x1539d8 and its resolve / constrain functions), the camera transitions and parameter blends, the direction and heading locks.

#### Rules
Those of C2.2, C2.4 and C2.5 apply. In addition:
- The game reads time only through `KGetExactTime` and input only through `cellPadGetData`: the replay feeds both there, never deeper in the game code.
- The hero's math is ported operation for operation (the replay compares to 1e-4 over 1800 frames, and differences grow).
- Recordings hold input, times and state numbers, no game bytes. They stay in `work/scratch-c26/` like the C2.4 / C2.5 captures.
- The hero code is the biggest unit of the game (1162 functions; 268 ran under control). Port by the call order of one frame, check with the replay after each group, and keep each `CKHkS08Hero_N.cpp` file to its original TU (the generated checklists).

### C2.7 The first fight

**Goal.** After control in LVL010, everything the original runs around and during the first fight works in `dotd.exe`, and the walk / fly / climb / fight replays match RPCS3:
- the fight's intro (the partner joins the fight, the slow motion) and the "Basic moves" tutorial pages;
- the golem attacking Warfang in the background, its thrown scrap and the walls it breaks;
- the enemies spawning in their fight zones, moving and attacking;
- the heroes' combos, breath and element attacks, hits both ways, bonuses dropped and picked up, fury, the dragon switch;
- the FX the fight shows (particles, lightning, explosions, sparks, shadow spots, trails, blur);
- the HUD (health, mana, combo, element gauges, messages);
- the hero's death and the restart at the save point.

**Out of scope (C2.8):**
- level scripting beyond the fight (doors, triggers, sequences, mechanisms, air currents as mechanisms, placed bonuses and pickups that no enemy drops);
- the golem *boss fight* (its QTE and later phases: `CKGolemPhaseQTE` ×3): only what the golem does in the background before it is C2.7;
- the pause menu, music, the level exit.
