#### Tools
The tool lists of C2.2–C2.6 apply. In particular, **the replay harness of C2.6 is how every step is checked:**
- `work/rpcs3_replay_record.py --skip-video --frames N --out DIR`;
- `newgame_test` with `DOTD_REPLAY` / `DOTD_REPLAY_OUT`;
- `work/replay_compare.py`.

Extend the recorder and the player with the fields this checkpoint makes comparable, in both `rpcs3_replay_record.py` and `dotd/tests/c15/newgame_replay.inc`:
- per enemy: id, node position, state, health;
- the enemy count per fight zone;
- per hero: health, mana, fury;
- the combo counter;
- the golem's phase and step;
- the live projectiles' count;
- the active bonuses' count.

Also `bones_compare.py` for the enemies' poses, and `frame_compare.py` for pixels (the C2.4 / C2.5 masks over characters and particles should be dropped piece by piece).

**rand:** the recorder writes the libc rand state per frame (C2.6). Make `replay_compare.py` report the first frame where it differs. Find the generator's algorithm from the libc `rand` the game links (`Rand_Int` 0x11500 → the TOC bridge → the library function), so the tool can say *how many* draws were missing or extra in that frame. Then find which caller made them: set a cdb breakpoint on dotd's `Rand_Int` with a stack, and compare it with the tracer counts of the callers in that frame's window.

#### Step 1. The fight's intro, the tutorial pages and the rand draws until the player can move
1. `CKS08MenuPauseInfo` (the tutorial pages: states, page flips, the continue input, title text) and whatever opens it at the fight's start: follow the messages from the fight intro to `CKS08GameManager_InterfaceStateDispatch`.
2. The partner's AICombat control state (20), the slow motion (`CKTimeScalingNodeFx_Update` 0x1d8108: the time scale ramp at +665..+694), `CKGrpS08Hero_UpdateTimers364_maybe`, and `IKGrpHero.cpp:137` (`CKGrpS08Enemy` vt+0xf0: whether a fight is on).
3. The rand draws until +1207: every per-frame `rand` caller the idle replay meets (the idle variation at +427 is a hero draw; the others come from the golem, FX, Sparx, townsfolk; see the rand tool above). Port each caller's draw first. The rest of the caller can follow in its step, but the draw count must match from now on.
4. **Exit:**
   - the idle replay (`work/scratch-c26/rec/idle`) matches every recorded field for all 1171 frames, the rand state included;
   - a new 1500-frame idle recording (user capture A) also matches to its end, and control is back at +1207 as in the original.

#### Step 2. The golem, its scrap and the walls it breaks
**Moved to C2.8 (2026-11-13), with evidence.** The recordings show no background attack before the boss sequence: `CKGolemPhaseAttack_Update` / `Start` and `CKGolemAttackCommon_*` first run at breath2 +7236 (0 calls in fight, fight2, fight3, idle1500-v8, death and breath2 before +7236), and the golem's idle per-frame calls draw no rand there (`work/reach-audit.json`, category c). The golem therefore goes with its boss fight in C2.8, including its restart chain (`CKHkGolem_Restart` 0x4bfa18, `CKHkS08DragonCityGolem_Restart` 0x4d5b38, the phase manager's / attack's vf60, `StopCurrentPhase`, `CKGolemAttackCommon_vf5C`, `CKHkGolem_MessageProc`), which needs the phase classes (incl. `CKGolemPhaseQTE`) and the condition classes. The tracer's earlier `combat` window with the golem attacking is that later moment. The rest of this step stays as written for C2.8.

1. The golem update, phases, attacks, steps and anim-mark triggers (listed above) as far as the background attacks before the boss fight go. The QTE phase is C2.8.
2. The projectile service and `CKProjectileTypeScrap` (launch, motion, orientation, collision, death) and the projectile part of `CKSrvCollision_ProcessDynamicVolumes` (`CKSrvCollision.cpp:565`), `CKDynBSphereProjectile`.
3. The crumbly zones: the messages that break them (`CKHkCrumblyZone_MessageProc` 0x40b500 → `Crumble` 0x408fe8), the multi-state zones, the pieces' motion, their ground sets (C2.6 ported the safe / broken sets), the active-actor list `CKHkActorBase_d82_maybe_OnActivate` 0x3f7338, the path blocking 0x9c1538. The 10 walls broken at control in the original must be broken at control natively, without the C2.4 "live hit points" diagnostic.
4. The golem's damage to the heroes (`CKS08GolemDamage`) through the damage path of step 4. Until step 4 is done, it calls the stub.
5. **Exit:**
   - the idle replays still match;
   - `replay_compare.py` shows the golem's phase / step and the projectile count equal every frame;
   - at control the crumbly zones' states equal the census;
   - a `DOTD_CAMERA_FROM` frame at control shows the same broken walls as the C2.4 RPCS3 capture (C2.4's control numbers 27.86 / 15.2 dB should improve).

#### Step 3. Enemies: spawning, moving, attacking
1. The fight zones and spawning: `CKGrpFightZone_*`, `CKS08FightZoneCpnt_Update`, `CSpawnManager` (`PreUpdate`, `UpdateAndExtract` = the spawn pools: the goblins on the ledges drawn as `CSpawnPoolRenderParam` says), `CKSpawnPool`, `CSpawnNode`, `CKS08GameSpawnPoint`.
2. `CKHkS08Enemy`: the state table and dispatch, `Update` and its per-frame calls (above), the animation layers (`IKHkEnemy.h:35`, `CKHkS08Enemy.cpp:196`: the layer object vtable 0xb22068 and the AnimBlendSlot at +0x04, over the C2.5 animation API), ground and transform, shadows, volumes, the status effects, the attack hit windows. Damage *reactions* come with step 4, but the states exist here.
3. `CKGrpSquad` (formation slots, spawn requests), `CKGrpS08Enemy`, `CKGrpS08PoolEnemy`, `CKS08CarrierEnemy` (its state machine), `CKS08EnemyAttack`, `CKS08EnemyElement` (+ pools), `CKEnemySectorCpnt`.
4. The AI around them: beacons, linked beacons, `NavSectors`, the behaviour blocks, the path finding left as straight segments (`CKPathFindingCinematicBloc.cpp:188` / `:264`).
5. The townsfolk and scripted NPCs that run every frame (`CKHkMovableCharacter`, `CKHkScriptedNPC`), as far as they run before the fight's end.
6. **Exit:**
   - with the walk recording (`work/scratch-c26/rec/walk`; the player walked into the fight) the enemies spawn at the same frames and places;
   - their positions and states match RPCS3 until the first frame where a hero hits one (from there it is step 4);
   - enemy bones at two frames match (`bones_compare.py`);
   - the goblins on the ledges are drawn.

#### Step 4. Hero combat, damage both ways, bonuses
1. The hero's combat states (the C2.6 tables' combat rows: action combos, HurtReaction, Recovery, Dead; animation ComboAnim, Hurt, HurtType1, GuardedHurt, Recovery, Dead; control AICombat for the partner) through the existing dispatch: replace the "not ported" side table entries.
2. Targets, combos (the `CKS08HeroCombo` trees and `CKS08HeroBlow`s), the hit spheres, lunges, grabs and throws, the combo time scale. Then the damage objects, from the hero to the enemies and from the enemies (and the golem) to the hero: `HandleHeroContactDamageAndImpulse` 0x30ce40 and its three stubs, `HandleHeroDamageAndReaction` 0x30d688, the enemies' contacts and damage state transitions, the status effects' damage.
3. The partner in combat (AI attack credits, targets, combat movement).
4. Bonuses dropped by enemies and the hero (`CKSrvBonus`, `CKGrpBonusPool`, the cone / star configs, gem rewards) and their pickup by the heroes (health, mana, gems, upgrade points). Placed bonuses that no enemy drops are C2.8.
5. **Exit:**
   - a *fight* recording (user capture B: melee only, the whole first fight) replays with the heroes', the enemies' and the bonuses' fields matching RPCS3 for every frame;
   - the fight ends at the same frame with the same enemy count, hero health and mana.

#### Step 5. Abilities, elements, fury, the dragon switch
1. The ability host and the ability state (action 85, animation Ability), the breath elements (`CKBreathElement`, `CKFireBreath`, `CKShadowBreath`), `CKProjectileElement`, the combo abilities in the census (`CKBlackCombo`, `CKPurpleCombo`, `CKShadowSurprise`), mana costs and cooldowns, the element levels, `UpdateRegenAndElementTimeout`'s element effects.
2. Fury: `UpdateFury`, `AddDragonFury`, the fury states, the HDR blend (`CKHDRNodeFx` messages).
3. The dragon switch and the co-op pieces moved from C2.6.
4. **Exit:** a *breath* recording (user capture C: fire breath and the other elements the heroes have, a dragon switch, fury if it fills) replays like step 4.

#### Step 6. The FX the fight shows
1. The FX service: pools, accessors, the particle components' emission queue, camera FX start / stop (`CKCamera.cpp`, `IKCamera.cpp`, `CKCameraClassicTrack3.cpp`, `CKCameraPathTrack.cpp` stubs), the game manager's IKFx slots and `SpawnSurfaceFx`.
2. Particle drawing: `CParticleGeometry`, render stages 13–15, the Im3D shader key 0xf07a8 and bind callback (in game the Im3D draws must use the uber-shader variant the original picks), the `STUB(C2)` pieces of `rpprtstd` / `rpptank` the level's emitters reach, `CFogBoxNodeFx`'s PTank setup and draw. Start from the C1.6 front-end path, which already draws particles. All particle nodes draw through this one pipeline, so do it once and check it on many.
3. The nodes that ran (lightning bolts, explosions, mesh pops, sparks, point lights, glows, shock waves, electric arcs, the target cursor, time scaling), the shadow spots, the trails (`CTrailNodeFx` and the hero's swing trails), the hero's FX stubs (footsteps, dust, water, partner link, flight FX).
4. Post effects moved from C2.4: blur, distortion, depth of field (`CKDOFNodeFx`). Point light 0 at control: check whether C2.4's yellower floor is its light.
5. Rand draws in FX code are part of step 1's count; the visual part is here.
6. **Exit:**
   - the C2.4 / C2.5 pixel comparisons are run **without the particle masks**: +120 and control (C2.5's numbers: +120 6.50 / 23.26 dB, control 27.73 / 15.12 dB, both with particles masked; report them unmasked now);
   - one new RPCS3 frame in the fight (user capture D) is compared with `DOTD_CAMERA_FROM` + the replay at the same frame;
   - the remaining differences are listed with their reason.

#### Step 7. The HUD
1. The four in-game interfaces (health, mana, gauges, combo display, upgrade text, shake, messages, visibility), the electric gauge, `CKFlashNode2dFx`, `CText2d_SetAlpha`, the selected-element HUD, the stat-change feedback, the center message.
2. The game manager's HUD pieces moved from C2.6: the hero / HUD message table 0x263c..0x270a and the dragon-statistics ids, combo timers and upgrade points (`CKCurve_Evaluate_maybe` 0x249ea8), best combo, boss gauge totals (needs the crumbly multi-state zones' health: `CKS08GameManager_5.cpp:396`).
3. The input icons in the world (`CKInputIconNodeFx`), the HUD shake (`ShakeEffect_Start_maybe` 0x24f770, the quake component's playback).
4. **Exit:** the HUD's gauge values in the replays equal RPCS3's (add them to the recorder). A capture-D frame shows the same HUD (by eye and `frame_compare.py` on the HUD regions).

#### Step 8. Death and restart
1. Game state 9 (`StateHeroDeath` in the game manager), the Dead states, the save points (`CKHkS08SavePoint`), `TeleportToSavedPosition`, the hazards (`ApplyHazardHitAndLeaveAttach`, `RequestOwnDragonSelectionIfMultiHero`), the restart path back to game state 7 (what is reset, what is kept).
2. **Exit:** a *death* recording (user capture E: let the heroes die in the fight, wait for the restart, walk a few seconds) replays to its end.

#### Step 9. The remaining `C2.7` sites and the C2.6 replays
1. `grep -rn "C2\.7" dotd/src dotd/tests`: port or move each line to C2.8 / a later level with its reason, as C2.6 step 7 did.
2. The replays C2.6 moved here: the *walk* recording (`work/scratch-c26/rec/walk`) and a *fly / climb* recording (user capture F, if C2.6 has none). Both must match to their end. Climbing: 57 of the 58 climb zones load after the first fight (a script, C2.8), so a climb recording can only reach the one zone loaded at the start.

#### Exit for C2.7 and verification
- `dotd.exe`: New game → (skip) → scene #13 → control → the intro, the tutorial pages, the first fight played with the gamepad (melee, breath, switch), won; also lost once with the restart at the save point. The HUD shows health, mana and combo; the golem breaks walls in the background; particles and lightning are drawn.
- Replays (idle, idle-1500, walk, fight, breath, death, fly / climb) match RPCS3 for every recorded field to their end, the rand state included. Write the numbers, and the first divergence of each if any, in this section's status.
- Pixels: +120, control and capture D compared unmasked; the differences listed with their owner.
- All tests green on both presets; `level_load_test` and the gates unchanged (the in-game gate's fields the fight code now writes match or get a named rule). Update this section's status and `HANDOFF.md`. Ghidra names go into `outputs/ability-analysis/corrections-c27.json` (verified, applied with `apply_corrections.py`, never saved).

#### Captures (ask the user)
All with `python work/rpcs3_replay_record.py --skip-video --frames N --out work/scratch-c27/rec/<name>`, RPCS3 closed beforehand, the gamepad in `--gamepad` mode as in C2.6. Each is needed only when its step starts:
- **A** (step 1) `idle1500`, `--frames 1500`: touch nothing. The tutorial pages stay open until the player presses: if the recorder needs the pages closed to reach +1207, press Cross only when they show, and say at which frame.
- **B** (step 4) `fight`, `--frames 5400`: close the tutorial pages, walk to the first fight and fight it with melee only (no breath, no switch) to its end.
- **C** (step 5) `breath`, `--frames 5400`: the same fight with fire breath and the other elements, one dragon switch, fury if it fills.
- **D** (step 6) a still frame in the fight with particles on screen: `python work/rpcs3_frame_capture.py --skip-video --frames control+N` with N from recording B (a frame with an explosion or lightning visible).
- **E** (step 8) `death`, `--frames 5400`: let the heroes die in the fight, wait for the restart, walk a few seconds.
- **F** (step 9, if C2.6 has none) `flyclimb`, `--frames 3600`: after the tutorial, double jump and fly, land, climb the climb zone near the start if there is one.
- **Still wanted** (HANDOFF): `python live_objects.py --level 10 --tag lvl010_ingame` right after control and the three `live_census.py` tags of §5 B. `lvl010_fight` is useful now: take it during capture B, in the middle of the fight.

#### Rules
Those of C2.2–C2.6 apply. In addition:
- Every `rand()` draw is ported where the original makes it, before anything else in its function is ported, so that the replays stay aligned.
- The fight is the most coupled system so far (hero, enemies, damage, FX, HUD and the golem all write each other's state). Port by the call order of one frame and check with the replays after each group, as in C2.6, with few agents at a time (the user's usage note).
- Recordings and captures stay in `work/scratch-c27/`, with no game bytes.

**Status, fight replay (2026-11-03).** `replay.sh RUN fight`: 1988 of 3956 frames have the same draw sequence; the first divergence is +1944, the first hit of the fight. The original hits only enemy 0x426c2 there and 0x626c2 one frame later (+1945); dotd hits both at +1944. Ruled out: the stored LTMs of the volumes (equal to fresh ones at the test), the swept-sphere test (`SphereVsSweptSphere` checked instruction by instruction, also by hand on the frame's numbers: every sweep start touches both enemies), the frustum culling (all four enemies posed), the armour parts (now ported: all of type 8, they never end a hit), the hit-sphere setup (`UpdateComboHitSpheres` / `SetupHitSphere` / `AttachVolumeToHotspot`). Facts that point elsewhere: in the original at +1944 `Contact_GetOtherVolume` runs 8 times (dotd 12; the 4 extra are 0x626c2's hurt volumes against the hero's body and hit sphere), `SphereVsSweptSphere` 18 times per frame from +1944 (dotd 8) and `ActivateTest` 52 times when the sphere is switched on (dotd 10). The 42 extra wakes are 30 owner-1 volumes (26 boxes, 3 type 3, 1 type 5: props the original enables at -244 / 0 / 484, dotd never does) and 12 volumes of the six pooled enemies. Next: find which props' volumes the original enables (-244: `CKGolemAttackCommon_AttachVolumesToHotSpots_maybe` runs 62 times then) and whether the awake list they add changes the pairs tested at +1944. The camera drifts by ~1e-6 from +1872 (the split follow spring starts there; the spring and its caller match the original), which does not change any pose at +1944.

**Status, fight replay (2026-11-03, evening): 2043 of 3956, first divergence +2005.** Fixed since: +1944 (the hero's `UpdateSwingTrails` 0x335158 was stubbed, so the trail states +0x1ad2 / +0x1ad3 never switched the combo to the trail hit spheres `SetupTrailHitSpheres2` / `4`: the 26 missing wakes), +1976 (`CKHkS08Enemy_BreakArmourPart`, the accessory launch / flight / break-off and the accessory manager's `PostLoad`, which sets the ground probe's delta 0.2 and height 2: without it a flying part fell through the ground). Also ported: `NavSteering_ComputeWallAvoidance_maybe` 0x2c2390 with `NavSteering_TestCellEdgeByNeighborFlags_maybe` 0x2bea90 and `NavSectors_PointToCell_maybe` 0x1e5148 (no change in the replay). **The RW frame model** now follows RW 3.7: `RwFrameGetLTM` syncs a hierarchy only when its root carries rwFRAMEPRIVATEHIERARCHYSYNCLTM (`_rwFrameSyncHierarchyLTM`: the frames under a SUBTREESYNCLTM mark recomputed, the marks cleared), else returns the stored LTM; `RwFrameUpdateObjects` / `RwFrameAddChild` keep the dirty frame list, synced at `RwCameraBeginUpdate`. Before, a loaded frame nobody asked for kept its creation LTM (the origin): the camera's push sphere and seven `CKCrumblyZoneState` spheres all sat at the origin, so `SphereVsSphere` reported full overlaps every frame, `ApplyPushSphereContact` pushed the follow camera 16 units down at the cinematic handover (+1838), the camera service's transition saw a 173 m/s velocity and cut the 2 s blend. The camera now matches bit for bit to +1871; from +1872 (the split follow spring after the hero's jump; spring and caller match the original instruction by instruction, so an input differs) it drifts by ~1e-5, and from +2058 the AI partner (whose stick is camera-relative) turns differently. **+2005:** the hero link bolts regrow there; bolt 3 (hero 0's hot spot 19, bone 5, anim 0x140 at 0.2399, inputs equal to the original's) is 1.99939 cells long in dotd, so its root gets 2 children (the original 3: at least 2.0). Forcing the length to 2.0006, 2.015 or 2.0333 (the previous frame's) all fix the frame, and no earlier regrowth tells the two apart. Needed: an RPCS3 read of the bolt's +0x30 (or the hot spot's world position) at +2005. With a forced length the next divergences are +2058 (the partner turn) and the camera drift. Since then: `PickAttackSpotNearHero` 0x280190 (three rand draws) with `HasSectorRoute` 0x1e9198 and `State0A_HitReaction` 0x2a9bc0 are ported (fight 2047/3956). Both recorders now also write each link bolt's `ends` (+0x30 start in node space, +0x3c end) and a `track` record (the follow camera's position, target, previous focus, spring speeds, flags; the service's flags and focus copy): **the next RPCS3 fight capture** (`rpcs3_replay_record.py --calls`, played) gives the original's values to compare frame by frame, which should settle the bolt length and the +1872 drift.

**Status (2026-11-04, late morning).** Capture 2 matches 2981 of 5950 frames (first divergence +2899), capture 3 4240 (+4155), capture 1 2244 (+2084); idle 2052/2052. `CKBlackBandBloc` is ported (Start: the shared bloc start, then by its mode every playing scene's entry or exit transition; capture 3's scene 12 now matches the original's element by element). **Branch senses:** the PPC `bge` / `ble` (and `bgelr` / `blelr`) without a `cror` are "not less" / "not greater" and are taken when the compare is unordered; ports must write them `!(a < b)` / `!(a > b)`, not `>=` / `<=`. `FloatApproach_Step` and `FloatApproach_StepAngle` were wrong this way: in the finisher pause (game dt 0) the hero's speed turn divides a zero angle by the zero dt, the original keeps its turn speed, dotd took the NaN and the hero's heading and velocity became NaN (capture 2 +2773). Found on the way: `CKIntegerCounter_MessageProc` 0x200230 was unported, so the tutorial trigger at +1765 (the counter's first loop) never fired; the level restart (CKLevel / input / sound / services / game manager / enemies / fight zones / hero / Sparx / crumbly zones / doors / pression stones / eggs / clamps / transportables / bonus pools / checkpoint snapshot Restart, 62+ functions) was unported, and the hero's missing Restart left the hero group's focus at 0xff (the comparator then read the MSVC guard bytes past the hero table); the clamp group's FX at the restart; the platform RWA stream players advanced by the clamped game dt after a restart (now real elapsed time); the fallback frame's render counter. **Reach audit** (`work/reach-audit.json` / `.md`, `work/reach_unported.py`): the replays only check draws and recorded state, so 503 functions the original runs in the six recordings had no `[x]`: 235 ported but unmarked, 44 platform by design, 130 later checkpoints (+7 golem rows moved there), 94 real C2.7 gaps, **all ported** (reports `work/c27-report-{reach-audit,sparx,gaps-levelstart,gaps-render,gaps-gameplay,gaps-gameplay2}.json`): among them the Sparx companion (OnActivate / Update never ported: it stood still) with `CKKLBCharacterSparx` and the linked beacon that gives it its target, the groups' PostLoad sector bit, the services' SetDefaultState, the hero's locked update in the fight-end cutscene (model fade, partner link bolt), the actor messages 0x908..0x90f with their overrides, the crumbly zones' MessageProc, the mushrooms' damage, zone / background node culling, the atomic pipeline setup, the message box. The fill test caught two unset fields this round: Sparx +0xbc (its OnActivate) and `CKHkSequenceMgr` +0xd4 (every live writer ported; the original reads its fresh heap 0: zeroed in the constructor; with junk the HUD vanished a frame after each unpause). Left as C2.8 stubs, never reached in C2.7 territory by any recording: the crumbly zone's Restore / TriggerCollapse, `CrumblyZoneState_ApplyDamage` / OnContact, `CKHkSequenceMgr_StopCurrentSequence` (no sequence starts), the bonus-orb updates (breath2 needs them after +6951). Gate (`aec9c21a`, the fill package's run on top of `b0d34dad`, which passed the main session's gate except breath2's fill): both presets 45/45; fight 3956, fight2 5950, fight3 5952, idle1500-v8 2052, death 4892 complete; breath2 through +6950; the fill test agrees on all six captures. **C2.7's exit still needs:** capture D (a still frame, for the Im3D key 0xf07a8 / 0xffde8). The new walk is dropped (2026-11-13): LVL010 cannot be walked from a new game without the first fight, and breath2 / death already cover the walking around and between the fights with today's recorded fields, frame for frame.

**C2.7 closed (2026-11-13).** The last stub site is ported (`a880fdef`, report `work/c27-report-im3d.json`): the Im3D shader key `ShaderVariantKey_BuildForImmediate3D_maybe` 0xf07a8 with `Immediate3DShaderPassState` and the bind callback 0xffde8 (installed through `RwDevice_SetIm3DShaderBindCallBack` 0x76b590), checked against capture D (`work/scratch-c27/capD`, RPCS3 still frames at control and control+300). Correction: the PTank particles do not use the Im3D key; `DrawPSGLParticleQuadBatch` 0x77aea8 calls the PTank hook 0xc4d708, which `InstallPSGLMeshCallbacks` sets to `PreparePSGLAtomicMeshShaders` (the mesh key of the PTank's first material); both paths are ported, plus `ParticleDraw_SetShaderParticleFlags_maybe` 0x88190 and the +0x8c store of `BindSelectedPSGLShaderPrograms` 0xfda80. Visible: the fogged PTanks (the lava glows and embers behind the arena wall) now take the level's fog ramp, as in RPCS3 (glow region at 430,160: original 117/66/24, before 154/79/24, after 113/64/24); every other pixel of the capture frames is unchanged. Open question for a later capture: `PreparePSGLAtomicMeshShaders` 0xff778 builds the key from `materials[0]` for every mesh it draws, so multi-material atomics would share material 0's key and colour in the original; dotd's mesh path uses each mesh's own material. **Exit state:** no `STUB(C2.7)` / C2.7 `DOTD_STUB_ONCE` left; both presets 45/45; fight 3956, fight2 5950, fight3 5952, idle1500-v8 2052 and death 4892 replay frame for frame; breath2 through +6950 (its C2.8 part starts at +6909); the fill test agrees on all six. C2.8 starts from breath2 +6909 (contextual mechanism), +7236 (the golem boss) and +8243 (the bonus orbs).

### C2.8 The rest of LVL010

**Goal.** LVL010 can be played in `dotd.exe` from New game to its end, as in the original:
- the level's mechanisms after the first fight;
- the golem boss with its QTE;
- the bonus orbs and collectibles;
- the scripted sequences, scripted cameras and NPCs;
- the climb zones and air currents that load later;
- music;
- saving at the save points;
- the end-of-level statistics screen, and the request and load of LVL020.

**The proof:**
- a full-level RPCS3 recording replays in dotd frame for frame, rand draws included (C2.6 / C2.7 harness);
- the reach audit of that recording finds no unported C2.8 function;
- the fill test agrees.

**Out of scope:**
- LVL020 and later levels beyond loading to control (C2.9 / phase 3);
- parity work that no LVL010 recording reaches (C2.9);
- things C2.7 moved to C2.9 with evidence: purple / black combo, shadow breath, transform B abilities not reachable, fog box modes 2 / 4, depth of field, the armour bonus pool, generic skinned geometry.

**Where C2.7 stopped** (C2.7 status lines, `work/reach-audit.md`, HANDOFF):
- **breath2.** The recording `work/scratch-c27/rec/breath2` (10550 frames after video_end, recorded with `--calls`) replays with the original's draw sequence through **+6950**. Its C2.8 part starts at:
  - **+6909**: the contextual mechanism;
  - **+7236**: the golem boss sequence;
  - **+8243**: the bonus orbs.

  The other six C2.7 recordings (fight, fight2, fight3, idle1500-v8, death; breath2 to +6950) replay completely and are the regression set.
- **The golem moved here with evidence.** No background attack exists before the boss sequence: `CKGolemPhaseAttack_*` and `CKGolemAttackCommon_*` first run at breath2 +7236. That includes its restart chain (`CKHkGolem_Restart` 0x4bfa18, `CKHkS08DragonCityGolem_Restart` 0x4d5b38, the phase manager's and attack's vf60, `StopCurrentPhase`, `CKGolemAttackCommon_vf5C`, `CKHkGolem_MessageProc`), the phase and condition classes (incl. `CKGolemPhaseQTE`) and the boss gauges.
- **Moved here by C2.7, never reached by a C2.7 recording:**
  - the crumbly zone's `Restore` 0x4085d8 / `TriggerCollapse` 0x407880, `CrumblyZoneState_ApplyDamage` 0x4047d0 / `OnContact` 0x404bc0, the zone collapse;

- **The stub list:** `grep -rn "C2\.8" dotd/src dotd/tests` gives 260 lines (2026-11-13). The main groups:

#### Tools
Everything from C2.6 / C2.7, with the C2.7 rules:
- **Iterate fast, gate slow.** Use `work/fast_replay.sh REC OUTDIR [ROOT]` for chases: x64 RelWithDebInfo `newgame_test`, about 13 s per recording. The x86 Debug parity replays (10–20 min) are only the commit gate.
- **Stubs first.** Before investigating a replay divergence, look at the replay's `stub:` lines and run `python work/reach_unported.py <rec>`. An unported function the original ran at that frame gets ported, not investigated.
- **Rand order.** `work/rand_diff.py` with the recording's `rands.jsonl` and dotd's `DOTD_REPLAY_RANDLOG` + `DOTD_REPLAY_RANDSYNC=1`: fix the first missing or extra caller in the frame order.
- **Unset fields:** `work/fill_test.py` on every recording (fills 00 / ff / 3f) catches what the replays miss.
- **Coverage:** `PORTING_PROGRESS.md` / `work/porting-progress.json`.
- **Reference:** `outputs/ability-analysis/level-scripting.md` (trigger → signal → target, cinematic blocs, message ids, the D82 hook slots), `outputs/remake/C27-*-BRIEF.md` (the brief style that worked).

2. **Exit:**
   - capture G replays to the frame the statistics screen opens, with the draw sequence and every recorded field;
   - the reach audit of G lists no unported C2.8 function (categories as C2.7: ported but unmarked, platform, later checkpoint with evidence);
   - the fill test agrees on G.

#### Step 5. Music and sound in the level
1. `CKSrvMusic` (12 open), `CKMusicPlayList` (19 open: the shuffled play lists draw rand, `CKMusicPlayList.h`), `CKDetectorMusic` (8 open), `CKHkMecaSound`, the sound emitters and `CKSoundOutputEffect` the level uses, on the C1.6 / C2.7 platform players (RWS music streams, RWA).
2. **Exit:**
   - the replays still match (music draws rand);
   - in `dotd.exe` the level's music starts, changes with the detectors and loops as in the original (checked by ear by the user, with the recording's timestamps for where it should change);
   - the replay harness's voice list matches where it records voices.

#### Step 6. Saving, the pause menu's rest
1. The save flow: `IKGameManager_UpdateSaveGameFlow_maybe` 0x140a8, `CKSavingManager` (25 open; `SaveObjects` 0x2c538: which objects write what), `CKS08GameManager_OnSaveGameDone_maybe` 0x4f8318, `CKS08MenuSelectSlot_OnSaveGameDone_maybe` 0x563720, the autosave option, and the save points (`CKHkS08SavePoint`).
   - **Platform:** a `platform/cell` save data layer replaces `cellSaveData`. The game's own buffer is written as it is, into a folder of the user's profile, never the game folder. Loading it is the C1.6 load-game path (`platform/cell/msgdialog`, the select-slot menu).
2. `CKS08MenuPause`'s 7 open lines, if capture G opens the pause menu in ways the C2.7 recordings did not.
3. **Exit:**
   - at capture G's save point frames, dotd's save buffer equals the game data RPCS3 wrote for the same save. Read RPCS3's save from `<RPCS3 folder>\dev_hdd0\home\00000001\savedata\` (`BLUS30205-SPYRO08-SAVE-<n>`, one folder per slot; slot 1 exists already, so note its date before G; compare the file the game writes, with the timestamps and PARAM.SFO left out);
   - in `dotd.exe`, saving at a save point, quitting, and Load game from the main menu puts the heroes at that save point with the same statistics.

#### Step 7. The end of the level and LVL020
1. The level's end sequence (its last cinematic scene and triggers: the end of capture G), `CKS08MenuStatsLevel` (the statistics screen: `OnMenuState` 0x56ab38, `RefreshStatTexts` 0x569f90, `HandleInput` 0x569138, `PollBackButton` 0x568fd8, `OnItemEvent` 0x5695a0, the title texts), the request of the next level (`RequestLevelChange` 0x4ec540, ported), `CKGameLoop_Mode9_LevelExit` 0x213c8 (ported), the OnLevelExit slots listed above, the unload path (`CKBundle_ShutdownHooks` 0x1dd788 ported; the services' SectorUnload / Stop pieces still stubbed: `grep -rn "SectorUnload\|Unload" dotd/src | grep -i stub`), then LVL020's load to control (the C2.0 loader, the C2.2 start path).
2. The unload check:
   - `level_load_test` must pass every run on both presets. Re-check the intermittent crashes (x64 Debug at the LVL110 → LVL120 switch, and at LVL010 → LVL020): with the unload path complete they are expected to go. If one stays, find the dangling pointer (Application Verifier / ASan preset `windows-msvc-asan`, the C1.6 tool).
   - In `dotd.exe` the memory after LVL010 → LVL020 is about what a fresh LVL020 load uses (no level leaked).
3. **Exit:**
   - capture G's last frames (the statistics screen, its input) replay;
   - leaving the statistics screen in dotd loads LVL020 to control;
   - the in-game gate for LVL020 (`objects_diff.py` against capture I, a live dump at LVL020's control) passes with named rules only;
   - `level_load_test` is clean in ten runs per preset.

`grep -rn "C2\.8" dotd/src dotd/tests` must list only items moved to C2.9 or a later level, each with its evidence ("not in LVL010": e.g. the water splash / wave, `CKFireBallNodeFx` in `CKProjectileTypeBallisticPFX`; or "no recording reaches it"), as C2.6 / C2.7 step 7 did. #### Captures (ask the user)
The C2.7 recording rules apply (memory "keyboard recording rules"):
- the user records on the **keyboard** (RPCS3's default pad: Enter START, X Cross, Q / E L1 / R1, R / T L2 / R2, arrows d-pad), so leave out `--gamepad`;
- record with `--calls` (the tracer and rand-log patches must be installed, as for breath2);
- RPCS3 closed beforehand, hands off until "play now";
- **no pause-menu cheats**.

- **G** (needed from step 4; ask for it at the start of C2.8 so it is ready): `python work/rpcs3_replay_record.py --skip-video --calls --frames 200000 --out work/scratch-c28/rec/full`.
  - Play LVL010 from control to its end: the tutorial and the first fight, the mechanisms, the golem boss, the sector-3 climbing, every save point (save once at each), collecting what is on the way.
  - Let the cinematics play (do not skip them).
  - Stop with Ctrl+C once the statistics screen has been left and LVL020's loading screen shows.
  - Write down roughly at what time each part happened (fight end, golem, saves, end).
  - If the recorder stops on a missed frame at a load, say at which moment: it needs a fix before G can be taken.
- **H** (step 2): `--frames 20000 --out work/scratch-c28/rec/golemdeath`: play to the golem boss and let the heroes die there once, wait for the restart, play a few seconds.
- **I** (step 7): at LVL020's control, `python live_objects.py --level 20 --tag lvl020_ingame` (the gate reference for LVL020) and `python live_census.py --tag lvl020_ingame`.
- **The saves of G:** copy nothing; tell where RPCS3 put them (the save data folder above) so step 6 can read them.
- **Still wanted** (HANDOFF): the LVL010 `live_objects.py` / `live_census.py` tags of §5 B, if not taken yet.

#### Rules
Those of C2.2–C2.7 apply ("iterate fast, gate slow", "stubs first", the rand order, the fill test). In addition:
- Every rand draw the later level makes (NPC wander, golem sounds and conditions, music play lists) is ported where the original makes it, first, so that G stays aligned.
- G is long, so chase with `fast_replay.sh` from a frame range: `replay_compare.py` reports the first difference, fix it, rerun. Keep the x86 Debug replay of G for the commit gate only.
- Save data and recordings stay out of git and out of the game folder (`work/scratch-c28/`, the user's profile), with no game bytes.

## 4. Agent organisation (same pattern as C1.4–C1.6)
- One brief per checkpoint (`outputs/remake/C2n-BRIEF.md`), families with disjoint files, a function pack per family (`work/c2n-packs/*.tsv`: generated like `work/c16_pack.py`, from the timeline phases + TU checklists), reports `work/c2n-report-<family>.json`.
- The main session: merges, runs both presets + gates + the all-levels load test, verifies and applies Ghidra names (`apply_corrections.py`, never `--save`).
- C2.1 (Loads) is the most parallel part (dozens of independent classes): many agents, one class family each.
- C2.4/C2.5 (rendering) and C2.6 (hero) are the critical path: few agents, the main session close by.

---

## 5. Captures needed from RPCS3 (the user runs these)

All scripts are in `work\` and only read guest memory. **A** needs the tracer boot patch on (RPCS3 → Manage Game Patches → "Spyro external quad module (experimental)" enabled; restart the game after enabling). **B** works with or without it.

**A. The LVL010 timeline with tagged windows (now; it scopes C2.2–C2.7).**
1. Enable the patch, start RPCS3 and go to the main menu (load no save).
2. In a terminal: `cd work` then `python tracer_timeline.py --tag lvl010_newgame --duration 1800` (it waits for the game; Ctrl+C ends early).
3. In the game's overlay (Select toggles its focus, L1/R1 switch windows), set the tracer **Tag**, then R3 starts/stops a window. Record these windows in this order, each tagged:
   - `cutscene`: start it right before choosing New game → Start story mode; stop when you have control. Do not skip the cutscene.
   - `idle`: stand still ~5 s.
   - `misc`: walk and run around ~10 s, jump a few times, move the camera.
   - `combat`: the first fight, start to end (melee only if you can).
   - `ability`: each breath/element the hero has at that point, one after another.
   - `pickup`: collect a few gems / health.
   - `checkpoint/save`: walk through a save point if there is one early.
   - `menu/pause`: open the pause menu, look at its pages, close it.
   - `death` (optional): let the hero die once and respawn.
   - `flying` / `climbing` / `fury` if the level offers them early.
4. At the end: `python tracer_probe.py --save` (the counters and the R3 windows), then Ctrl+C the timeline.
5. Tell me roughly what you did in each window (one line each) and anything unusual.

**B. Censuses at fixed moments (now; the reference for C2.1–C2.2).** With the game running (patch on or off):
- during the opening cutscene: `python live_census.py --tag lvl010_cutscene`
- right after control is given, before moving: `python live_census.py --tag lvl010_ingame`
- during the first fight: `python live_census.py --tag lvl010_fight`

**Later (after C2.0 extends the tool):** `python live_objects.py --tag lvl010_ingame` at the same "control given" moment (the gate-2 reference for C2.1/C2.2). I will ask when it is ready.

**Not needed now:** screenshots (pixel comparison comes later), more front-end captures.

---

## 6. Generalising to the next levels
1. **Classes:** after LVL010, 37 classes remain over all levels (§1.2). Each level's checkpoint starts with `kp3-class-usage.json`: the classes it adds, their Loads (C2.1 process), their runtime from that level's timeline.
2. **One test for all levels:** `level_load_test` (C2.0) loads every LVLnnn and its sectors headless and must stay green; later a `level_start_test` brings each level to game state 7 at its first spawn point.
3. **Per-level capture recipe:** the same as §5 A/B with `--tag lvlNNN_*`, run once per level before porting it.
4. **Per-level timeline packs:** `work/c16_pack.py`-style generator parameterised by tag, listing the functions that level runs first that no earlier level ran (most will be none after LVL010 + 020).
5. **Data-only differences** (new textures, sounds, cinematics, videos) need no code; new mechanics (flying levels, bosses: `CKS08Malefor*`, golem phases, turrets) are their own small checkpoints inside the level that introduces them.
6. **Order:** LVL010 → LVL020 → … in game order, since each level's start depends on the previous one's exit and saved state.
